# Seeds without evidence

<sub>[fastpp](../README.md) › [Catalog](README.md)</sub>

Projects mined from curated lists (see `data/seeds/seeds.json`) for which a backfill seed-record agent
found no numeric comparison against any recognisable baseline. They are not catalogued; they are kept
here so a later wave or a human can revisit them. Generated by `pipeline/seeds_no_evidence.py`.

620 projects.

| Project | Repository | Listed as | Why no record | Pass |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8cc | https://github.com/rui314/8cc | A Small C Compiler. | README states 'Generated code is usually 2x or more slower than GCC' and 'It's not an optimizing compiler'; project is archived (successor chibicc) and makes no claim of being faster than any named baseline. No benchmark numbers, no shootout where it wins. | w2-mine-a |
| Abseil Logging | https://github.com/abseil/abseil-cpp | The Abseil Logging library provides facilities for writing log messages to stderr, files, or other sinks. | No numeric comparison against any baseline anywhere reachable. absl/log/log_benchmark.cc only measures its own overheads (disabled/enabled LOG, CHECK, VLOG_IS_ON) with no glog, spdlog or std::cerr counterpart; the docs page abseil.io was unreachable (ENOTFOUND). Third-party logging shootouts found (MuggleWei/cc_log_benchmark: easyloggingpp, fmtlog, glog, haclog, loguru, nanolog, quill, reckless, spdlog; spdlog README; RafaGago/logger-bench) do not include absl::log. | w11-mine-e |
| ACE | https://github.com/DOCGroup/ACE_TAO | An OO Network Programming Toolkit in C++. | No numeric comparison reachable. The ACE_TAO repo page and ACE/performance-tests/README only list benchmark programs (Synch-Benchmarks, TTCP with C sockets versus ACE wrappers, UDP, Misc) with no committed results; ACE/apps/JAWS is an ACE-based web server. The only comparative claim found is directional, from WebSearch snippets of the Hu, Pyarali and Schmidt JAWS papers (a USENIX NT 1997 paper and the Schmidt-site PDF globalinternet.pdf): JAWS 'consistently outperforms existing Web servers, such as Apache, Java Server, PHTTPD, Zeus, and Netscape Enterprise' over 155 Mbps ATM on a dual-CPU 180 MHz UltraSPARC 2. The papers themselves (usenix.org, dre.vanderbilt.edu, ftp.icm.edu.pl) are EGRESS_BLOCKED here, so no numbers could be read; the listed project page https://www.dre.vanderbilt.edu/~schmidt/ACE.html is also blocked. Lead for a session with paper access: retrieve the JAWS paper and record JAWS (ACE) versus Apache/Zeus as a tier B entry (peer-reviewed, 1997 hardware). | w5-mine-f |
| aes-stream | https://github.com/jedisct1/aes-stream | A fast AES-based stream cipher for C. | No numbers anywhere reachable. README says only 'simple, but fast', 'designed to fill large buffers' and 'Clang 7 appears to produce faster code than gcc 8' (no figures); repo holds only src/aes-stream.c and aes-stream.h, no benchmark. It is an AES-PRF random number generator with fast key erasure (AES-NI required), not a stream cipher as the list says. Two WebSearches found no third-party throughput numbers. 21 stars, BSD-2-Clause per GitHub (list tag says ISC), last commit 2023-07-15. | w7-mine-a |
| AGENT++ | https://www.agentpp.com/api/cpp/agent_pp.html | C++ framework provides a complete tri-lingual SNMP v1/2c/3 protocol engine and dispatcher for the development of SNMP agents. | No numeric comparison found. agentpp.com is EGRESS_BLOCKED, github.com/agentpp shows no public repositories, and a WebSearch for AGENT++ benchmarks against net-snmp returned only package listings (Buildroot, Fedora copr, AUR) that describe features (SNMPv1/v2c/v3 engine, Apache-2.0 licence) with no performance claims. | w5-mine-f |
| Amplitude Audio SDK | https://github.com/SparkyStudios/AmplitudeAudioSDK | A cross-platform audio engine designed with the needs of games in mind. | README (opened) has no performance claim or numbers. No published benchmark against FMOD or Wwise found (WebSearch returned only Wwise/FMOD profiling guidance and nothing on Amplitude). | w10-mine-e |
| ANNetGPGPU | https://github.com/ANNetGPGPU/ANNetGPGPU | A GPU (CUDA) based Artificial Neural Network library. | No comparison against a recognizable baseline. The README claims only 'speed-ups by a factor of 100 ... for bigger networks (>256x256 nodes)' of its own CUDA self-organizing map (SOM) versus its own quad-core CPU/OpenMP SOM, plus Figure 1 (an image, no numbers) comparing training progress on a quad-core CPU and a GTX 570 in fixed time. Nothing against TensorFlow, PyTorch or any other library: README has no other numbers, no benchmark directory (root: src, CMakeLists, Doxyfile), issue search for benchmark, tensorflow, pytorch or speed returns 0 results. Last activity 2017; GitHub shows GPL-3.0 (awesome-cpp tag says LGPL), 113 stars. | w12-mine-a |
| Apache C++ Standard Library | https://github.com/apache/stdcxx | STDCXX, A collection of algorithms, containers, iterators, and other fundamental components. | No numeric comparison reachable. The GitHub mirror (README, tests/ with no perf or bench directory, last commit 2012-09-15) has no benchmark data; stdcxx.apache.org, the Apache JIRA and the Apache wiki are blocked here (EGRESS_BLOCKED), and search snippets only say 'excellent runtime performance' without numbers. A snippet-only hint of an old stringstream test (gcc 3.2.3, 1.9GHz P4) had stdcxx slower than gcc. | w1-mine-j |
| apophenia | https://github.com/b-k/apophenia | Library for statistical and scientific computing. (awesome-c#Numerical, listed with http://apophenia.info, GPL-2.0-only) | Same project as the b-k/apophenia seed below. Only a directional claim, no numbers and no named baseline: README says 'Being in C, it is often an order of magnitude faster when searching for optima or running MCMC chains'. The listed site http://apophenia.info is unreachable from this environment (getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND); a web search found no benchmark or timing study. | w8-mine-g |
| APR | https://github.com/apache/apr | Apache Portable Runtime; another library of cross-platform utility functions | No numeric comparison found. README says only 'without sacrificing performance' and 'high performance allocators'; the mirror has no benchmark files and two rounds of web search for apr_palloc or apr_hash against malloc or std containers found no published numbers (pools are built on malloc and are documented for convenience, not raw speed). | w1-mine-j |
| Argh! | https://github.com/adishavit/argh | A minimalist, frustration-free, header-only argument handler. | README and repo page contain no benchmarks or speed claims versus getopt or Boost.Program_options; the pitch is simplicity and no dependencies, not speed. | w2-mine-a |
| Argon2 | https://github.com/P-H-C/phc-winner-argon2 | The password hash Argon2, winner of PHC. (awesome-cpp Cryptography) | No numeric comparison against any named baseline. The README 'Benchmarks' section (make bench) prints only Argon2d/Argon2i absolute cycles per byte and seconds for 1 MiB to 4096 MiB (for example 4096 MiB, 2 threads: 2.15 cpb, 13.0 s); CHANGELOG.md has no speed figures. The checked-in specification (argon2-specs.pdf, read via raw.githubusercontent.com) gives Table 4 (Argon2d 1.3 cpb at 1 thread, 0.6 cpb at 4 threads on an i7-4500U, gcc 4.8.2 -O3 -mavx) and says Argon2 'equals in performance with the PHC finalists' and Argon2d is about three times as fast as Argon2i, but it names scrypt, bcrypt, PBKDF2, Catena and Lyra2 only qualitatively and compares no timings. Directional claims only. | w7-mine-c |
| argparse | https://github.com/cofyc/argparse | Command-line argument parsing library, inspired by Python's argparse module. | No performance claim at all. README and FAQs describe usability against getopt; no benchmark, no numbers, no speed comparison anywhere in the repo. | w1-mine-j |
| argparse | https://github.com/p-ranav/argparse | Argument Parser for Modern C++. | README (read in full), repo page and a web search show no benchmark or performance comparison against getopt, Boost.Program_options or anything else. No numbers anywhere reachable. | w3-mine-d |
| Argy | https://github.com/mshenoda/argy | Command-Line Argument Parsing Library for Modern C++ - Simple, Intuitive, and Header-only with Zero Dependencies | README and repo page (no benchmark directory) contain no performance claim or numbers; web search found no third-party comparison. | w3-mine-d |
| ArrayFire | https://github.com/arrayfire/arrayfire | A general purpose GPU library. | Repo README claims only 'Rigorous benchmarks and tests ensuring top performance' and 'near-optimal kernels' with no numbers; docs and release notes (pages/release_notes.md) hold no baseline comparison. The only comparison found is ArrayFire's own 2014-era blog post 'Benchmarking parallel vector libraries' (ArrayFire 3.0 vs Boost.Compute, Bolt, TBB, Thrust on K20, FirePro S10000, Xeon E5-2560v2), which is on arrayfire.com (ENOTFOUND here, never opened) and per search snippets shows mixed results (Thrust faster than ArrayFire on K20 above 1M elements). Directional claim only, no openable numbers. | w11-mine-e |
| asnlc (asn1c, Lev Walkin) | https://github.com/vlm/asn1c | Compiler of ASN.1 specifications into C source code. | No claim that asn1c is faster than anything. The repo README (BSD-2-Clause, 1.2k stars) has no performance statements, and lionet.info is EGRESS_BLOCKED. The only numeric comparison reachable is third-party and shows asn1c slowest: eerimoq/asn1tools examples/benchmarks/c_source README encodes and decodes one PDU 1,000,000 times (UPER): asn1tools 1.229 s at -O3 and 2.987 s at -Os, asn1scc 4.023 s and 6.166 s, asn1c 10.902 s and 12.569 s; asn1c executable text 63733 bytes versus 5553 (asn1tools) and 12073 (asn1scc), and asn1c uses dynamic memory and supports all codecs. The list's guessed category (http-servers-frameworks, baseline standard HTTP server) is wrong; the project is an ASN.1 codec generator (serialization-and-formats). The similarly named Objective Systems ASN1C benchmark pages (obj-sys.com) are for a different, commercial product. | w5-mine-f |
| assimp | https://github.com/assimp/assimp | Open Asset Import Library (assimp) is a cross-platform 3D model import library which aims to provide a common API for different 3D asset file formats. | Readme.md (opened) has no speed claim. Third-party comparison (Aras Pranckevicius, .obj parse libraries, 2022; page blocked here, snippet only) says assimp is not fast by design (its site says it is not designed for speed) and slower than tinyobjloader, fast_obj and rapidobj. Rapidobj docs/BENCHMARKS.md (opened) has no assimp row. It is a slow baseline, not a faster alternative. | w10-mine-e |
| Async++ | https://github.com/Amanieu/asyncplusplus | A lightweight concurrency framework for C++11, inspired by the Microsoft PPL library and the N3428 C++ standard proposal. | No benchmark or performance claim in README, wiki (Home, Tasks, Parallel algorithms, Schedulers), issues or repo tree (examples, include, src only). Not included in the tzcnt/runtime-benchmarks shootout (citor, libfork, TooManyCooks, tbb, taskflow, cppcoro, coros, HPX, concurrencpp, libcoro, cobalt); no other shootout naming Async++ was found. | w11-mine-e |
| Au | https://github.com/aurora-opensource/au | A C++14-compatible physical units library with no dependencies and a single-file delivery option. README says 'no runtime performance penalty' with no figures. docs/alternatives/index.md has a 'Compilation Speed' row comparing nholthaus 2.x/3.x, Boost.Units, bernedom/SI, mp-units and Au, but only as qualitative ratings (Au 'Good: not subjectively noticeable'; nholthaus 2.x 'very slow (adds multiple seconds)') plus ratios between competitors (nholthaus 3.x default about 4x the 2.x penalty, mp-units default 2 to 3 times nholthaus, bernedom/SI roughly 2/3 the penalty of Au and Boost) and the page states Au is slower than bernedom/SI; no Au timings. Repo benchmarks/ holds compile-time benchmark sources (BUILD.bazel, defs.bzl) but no published results; issue #759 and PR #799 contain no numbers in the fetched text. The lead's guessed baseline (manual unit conversion) has no benchmark anywhere. Category guess big-integer-arbitrary-precision is wrong: it is a physical-units library. | w8-mine-a |
| AudioFile | https://github.com/adamstark/AudioFile | A simple C++ library for reading and writing audio files. | Directional only: release note 1.0.9 says 'Faster loading of audio files' with no numbers and no named baseline. | autodiff | https://github.com/autodiff/autodiff | A modern, fast and expressive C++ library for automatic differentiation. | Own README only says 'efficient'; no benchmark numbers. The only numbers found are third-party and show it slower, not faster: buech/ad-benchmarks (Xcelerit, maker of XAD; GCC 13.3, Xeon Platinum 8488C, -O3 -mavx2 -mfma) gives SABR calibration gradient 38 ms for autodiff (forward dual mode) vs CppAD 32 ms, Adept 19 ms, XAD 8.4 ms; autodiff did not finish the Heston MC, XVA CVA and LIBOR swaption benchmarks. If the orchestrator wants a flagged not-faster record, those are the numbers. | w8-mine-a |
| avcpp | https://github.com/h4tr3d/avcpp | Modern C++ wrapper around FFmpeg. | Wrapper around the FFmpeg C API. README and repo tree (opened) have no performance claim, no benchmark directory and no numbers; its goal is API convenience, not speed. | w10-mine-e |
| barkeep | https://github.com/oir/barkeep | Small C++ header to display async animations, counters, and progress bars. | No numeric comparison anywhere reachable: README has no benchmarks or overhead figures (only 'Small' and 'minimally intrusive' wording, speed settings in the examples are display rates), the repo has no benchmark directory (barkeep/, docs/, python/, tests/), the issue search for performance/overhead/benchmark returns nothing, and a web search found no third-party overhead comparison against indicators, progressbar or tqdm. Lead claim word 'Small' is about size, not speed. | w11-mine-a |
| Bcrypt | https://bcrypt.sourceforge.net/ | A cross platform file encryption utility. Encrypted files are portable across all supported operating systems and processors. No benchmark or comparison against another encryption tool reachable. | w7-mine-c |
| BerkeleyDB | https://github.com/berkeleydb/libdb | Library for a high-performance embedded database for key-value data. | No verifiable numeric win over a named baseline. Oracle's own SQLite-comparison whitepaper (oracle.com bdb-sqlite-comparison-wp-176431.pdf) and the Oracle SQL API article returned HTTP 403, so the claimed figures (search-result summary only: about as fast as SQLite for reads, about 3x on updates) could not be read from a page I opened. The community fork archive (berkeleydb/libdb, Sleepycat license; Oracle 6.x+ is AGPL-3.0) only reports improvement against its own earlier code (shared-handle random reads 174k to 3.7M ops/s at 24 threads on 96 vCPUs) and its test/bench/CROSS-ENGINE-2026-09.md study shows libdb 5.3.37 behind WiredTiger by 4.5x to 58x; the regression harness README itself disclaims any absolute comparison with InnoDB, WiredTiger or LMDB. The LMDB microbench (lmdb.tech) that would show BDB as the slower engine is blocked. | w6-mine-a |
| Better Enums | https://github.com/aantron/better-enums | Reflective enums (to string, iteration). DesignDecisionsFAQ.md and OptInFeatures.md only compare the library with its own earlier data-structure attempts. No comparison with magic_enum, a hand-written switch, std::string_view tables or another enum library found in the repo, docs, or search (pistack.xyz and feedbagel.com posts were blocked). | w4-mine-g |

| bit7z | https://github.com/rikyoz/bit7z | A C++ static library offering a clean and simple interface to the 7-zip shared libraries. | No numbers anywhere reachable. The wiki lists API pages (Basic Usage, Advanced Usage, Building) and no performance page among the roughly 44 titles shown (29 more were hidden by the page and not checked). | Blackhole | https://github.com/3Hren/blackhole | Attribute-based logging framework, which is designed to be fast, modular and highly customizable. | Directional claims only. | Bolt | https://github.com/HSA-Libraries/Bolt | A C++ template library optimized for GPUs. | Directional claim only ('high-performance library implementations', 'performance and power efficiency benefits'). The only numbers found are third-party and not in Bolt's favor: a search snippet of a 2016 sort/scan library comparison for GPUs (arXiv 1601.03144, blocked here) says Thrust, Bolt and Boost.Compute show no advantage for sorting and scanning and Boost.Compute beat HSA-Bolt on AMD FirePro S10000; a 2013 blog post on CPU vs GPU sorting (solarianprogrammer.com) is blocked. | Boost.Beast | https://github.com/boostorg/beast | HTTP and WebSocket built on Boost.Asio in C++11. | No numeric comparison against websocketpp or any other library. The websocketpp comparison page (doc/qbk/08_design/3_websocket_zaphoyd.qbk) is design-only (type requirements, shared_ptr use, type erasure). The README states performance as a goal ('thousands of connections') with no numbers. PR threads #266, #353 and #453 carry no timings. Directional claim, no numbers against a baseline. | w10-mine-f |

| Boost.PropertyTree | https://github.com/boostorg/property_tree | A property tree parser/generator that can be used to parse XML/JSON/INI/Info files. The only numbers found show it losing: facebook/osquery issue 3164 (2017, no hardware or compiler given) has boost::ptree DB_serialize_column_order at 3,493 ns vs RapidJSON 871 ns (4.0x slower), 23,099,589 ns vs 1,388,649 ns at 100/100 (16.6x slower); boostorg/property_tree issue 105 is titled 'read_json takes a long time'. Searches of danielaparker/json_benchmarks, stephenberry/json_performance and mloskot/json_benchmark found no property_tree rows. | w4-mine-a |

| C++ Actor Framework | https://github.com/actor-framework/actor-framework | An Open Source Implementation of the Actor Model in C++. | No numbers reachable. README, repo docs (doc/pdf holds only design diagrams) and the actor-framework/benchmarks repo README (microbenchmark suite, no results, no licence shown) carry no figures. Real baselines would be Erlang, Akka, Charm++ and other actor runtimes, not libevent or standard async I/O, and the category is actor and concurrency runtime rather than event-loops-io. Lead: Charousset, Hiesgen, Schmidt 'Revisiting Actor Programming in C++' (Computer Languages, Systems & Structures 2016, arXiv 1505.07368); a WebSearch snippet of its abstract says CAF outperforms Erlang, Charm++, SalsaLite, Scala, ActorFoundry and OpenMPI up to 64 cores at low memory, but the paper itself (arxiv.org, ar5iv.labs.arxiv.org, reposit.haw-hamburg.de, inet.haw-hamburg.de, hgpu.org) is EGRESS_BLOCKED here so no number could be read. Worth a retry from a session with paper access; would be a B record on the CAF benchmarks repo plus the paper. | w5-mine-g |
| C++ REST SDK | https://github.com/Microsoft/cpprestsdk | C++ REST SDK (previously named Casablanca). | No claim of being faster and no numbers in the repo (archived 2026-06-01; README now recommends libcurl or Boost.Beast). The only numeric comparison found is the independent shootout https://github.com/guteksan/REST-CPP-benchmark (issue #1432), where cpprestsdk v2.10.15 is the slowest or ties: 3.401 s against Crow 1.536 s, Pistache 1.059 s, Restbed 1.333 s, Restinio 1.025 s, cpp-httplib 3.015 s (10,000 POSTs, t=0 ms, local, 28 threads), and it deadlocks when service threads are fewer than client producers. | w5-mine-g |
| C++ wfrest | https://github.com/wfrest/wfrest | C++ Web Framework REST API. | README only says 'Fast, efficient' with no numbers. benchmark/ holds wfrest and libhv echo servers plus wrk commands but no results. docs/benchmark_results.md compares wfrest's own CachedFile serving with its own normal static serving (2.0x to 3.8x, wrk) which is a self comparison, not a named external baseline. docs/static_file_optimization.md cites issue #272 that default static file serving is up to 4 times slower than a regular Apache setup for small files, i.e. evidence against being faster. Sogou Workflow (the engine underneath) publishes its own nginx comparisons but that is a different project. | w5-mine-g |
| C++React | https://github.com/schlangster/cpp.react | A reactive programming library for C++11. | No numbers against any external baseline. benchmarks/src (Grid, Sequence, Fanout, Random, LifeSim) only compare its own propagation engines. README says it can profile live to decide when parallel updates pay off, with no figures. Wiki page 'Manual updates vs. Signals' is qualitative. Master branch is mid-rewrite; legacy1 README has no benchmark. WebSearch found no third-party comparison. | w11-mine-f |
| c-ares | https://github.com/c-ares/c-ares | A C library for asynchronous DNS requests. | No benchmark in README, RELEASE-NOTES.md, docs or test/ (no benchmark files; ares_queryloop.c is a query-loop tool). The only numbers found are against it: issue #500 'Under load, ares_getaddrinfo is 3x slower than libc getaddrinfo (w/ threadpool)', 50 simultaneous lookups on an Apple M1 Max under bun 0.4.1: libc 749.81 ms/iter, c-ares 2.38 s/iter, macOS getaddrinfo_async 176.99 ms/iter (reporter found two small socket writes per call for AF_UNSPEC; closed Jan 2023, resolution not visible). The directional claim that c-ares is faster than getaddrinfo in Node.js docs has no numbers; c-ares.org mailing list archives with 2007 timings were EGRESS_BLOCKED. | w5-mine-g |
| c-capnproto | https://github.com/jmckaskill/c-capnproto | Implementation of the Cap'n Proto serialization protocol. | No numeric comparison anywhere reachable. The repo (archived 2023-11-04, MIT, 69 stars) has no README and no benchmark file (root: compiler, capn.c, capn.h, capn-malloc.c, capn-stream.c, tests); all 7 issues are install, usage and compiler-warning reports, none about speed or msgpack. A web search found only Cap'n Proto C++ comparisons against protobuf, not this C implementation against msgpack or anything else. | w1-mine-k |
| C-Macro-Collections | https://github.com/LeoVen/C-Macro-Collections | Generate simple and generic data structures using macros. | Harness but no numbers. The repo has benchmarks/benchmark.c (CMC hashtable and heap timings, no baseline) and benchmarks/comparisons/hashtable (hashmap.c and hashmultimap.c against unorderedmap.cpp, a std::unordered_map with MurmurHash, built with -O3 and timed with hyperfine at COUNT=20000000), but no result numbers appear in the README, the mdBook docs index (searchindex.json has no benchmark, unordered or C++ mentions) or the issues; issue #10 says the examples and benchmarks are outdated. README only has design claims (array-based structures are faster due to caching). Directional only, so no record. | w1-mine-k |
| C-Turtle | https://github.com/walkerje/C-Turtle | A C++11 header-only turtle graphics library acting as a CImg wrapper. | No benchmark of any kind in README or repo page. | CarpVM | https://github.com/tekknolagi/carp | "interesting" VM in C. Let's see how this goes. | No numeric comparison anywhere reachable and no speed claim at all. The repo tree (examples, extras, fuzz, src, tests, TODO.md, SYNTAX.md) has no benchmark directory; the repo page shows GPL-3.0, 984 stars, archived by the owner on 2021-01-19. | w12-mine-d |
| cchan | http://repo.hu/projects/cchan/ | Small library for channel constructs for inter-thread communication. | No numeric comparison reachable. repo.hu and the 2010 suckless dev-list announcement were EGRESS_BLOCKED; a web search snippet describes a very small mutex and condition-variable channel library for pthreads and SDL threads with select() planned, and mentions no benchmark. | CGAL | https://github.com/CGAL/cgal | Collection of efficient and reliable geometric algorithms. | No first-party numeric comparison against a named external baseline reachable. Checked CGAL manual sources in the repo (Triangulation_3, Spatial_searching, Convex_hull_2/3, Surface_mesh, Polygon_mesh_processing, Kernel_23, Mesh_3, Orthtree, Isosurfacing_3 and about 40 more) and Installation/CHANGES.md: the benchmarks there are CGAL against itself (kernels, sequential versus parallel, bucket sizes) or give no numbers (Orthtree 'faster than a kd-tree', Marching Cubes 'substantially faster' than Delaunay refinement, 'efficient'). Triangulation_3 manual points to Liu and Snoeyink MSRI 2005 benchmarks comparing CGAL to other 3D Delaunay software, but that paper could not be opened. Search snippets of VQhull (arXiv 2510.09417: CGAL Quickhull 0.61 s versus Qhull 1.1 s on a 10^7-point disk) and ParGeo are third-party papers with CGAL as one comparison row; arXiv is blocked here, so no page with those numbers was opened. | w1-mine-a |
| ChaiScript | https://github.com/ChaiScript/ChaiScript | An easy to use embedded scripting language for C++. [BSD] [website](https://chaiscript.com/) | Every number found shows ChaiScript slower than the recognizable baselines, and its maintainer says so. Scriptorium (r-lyeh-archived/scriptorium README and bench.csv, 2015, AMD A10 3.8 GHz, VS2013/2015, recursive fib): ChaiScript 175.038 s versus PUC Lua 1.341 s, Duktape 9.544 s, MicroPython 2.842 s; it is ahead only of 42tiny-js (227.170 s) and lil (555.976 s), which are not recognizable baselines. Gist totalgee/640805cb8995cf588152 (2015, Mac Pro Xeon E5 3.7 GHz, loop sum): ChaiScript 0.1898 to 0.4949 s versus Lua 5.3.0 0.0098 s, Python 2.7.6 0.0413 s, Node.js 0.0028 s. ChaiScript issue #189: ChaiScript 5.7.0 4.73 s versus Lua 5.1 0.36 s (sum), 31.01 s versus 0.35 s (primes), and the maintainer replies 'ChaiScript will never be as fast as Lua or JavaScript at math' and 'ChaiScript excels at calling C++ functions, not at math'. The README (opened, readme.md) and the performance_tests directory (profile scripts only, no comparison) make no speed claim against another engine; the only gain the maintainer cites is 'almost 100x faster than ChaiScript 1.0', a self comparison. Florian-Thake/BenchmarkScripts-TeaScript-VS-ChaiScript-VS-Jinx links results on blocked articles. BSD licence, 3.1k stars. | w12-mine-d |
| Channel | https://github.com/andreiavrammsd/cpp-channel | Thread-safe container for sharing data between threads. | Repo benchmarks/channel_benchmark.cpp (Google Benchmark, GCC 13.3.0, 8-thread CPU, results in a header comment) times only its own queue, vector and array storages against each other (for example 100000-element string inputs: queue_storage 652607002 ns, vector_storage 974087950 ns, array_storage 628774895 ns). No std::thread plus mutex baseline, no other channel library. README and docs make no performance claim. | w11-mine-f |

No third-party shootout including csv2 was found (checked csv-parser's benchmarks/README.md, dfglib csvPerformanceRuns.md, MIOCSV, CSV_co READMEs and WebSearch). Only performance remark is a README sentence that plugging in fast_float 'allows for improving the performance of parsing floating point numbers compared to the standard library', with no numbers. No third-party shootout including Cuba as the faster library was reachable. | w8-mine-h |
| cuda-api-wrappers | https://github.com/eyalroz/cuda-api-wrappers | Lightweight, Modern-C++ wrappers for the CUDA GPU programming runtime API. | No numeric comparison anywhere reachable. Issue #279 is about removing runtime-API use, with no timings; an issue search for overhead, benchmark and performance found no wrapper-versus-raw-CUDA timings. A web search found no third-party benchmark of the library. BSD-3-Clause, 906 stars, default branch master. | w11-mine-g |

That README and doc/TuningGuide.md, doc/PortingGuide.md and CHANGELOG.md say only that a find-and-replace CUDA port runs 'rather bad' on x86 and that, with a compile-time element count N=1, CUDA code 'will just stay with the very same performance' (qualitative, no numbers). Search snippets show alpaka (the backend, a separate project) reporting near-zero overhead against native CUDA and OpenMP in its 2016 paper (arXiv 1602.08477) and a DGEMM result, but arXiv is blocked here and those numbers are for alpaka, not cupla. | w11-mine-g |

It is a thin object-oriented wrapper over libcurl, so the baseline it could beat is libcurl itself, and no wrapper-overhead or speed claim exists. A search for wrapper-overhead benchmarks versus raw libcurl found only a curl mailing-list remark that bindings should rarely cost significant speed, no numbers. | w3-mine-e |
| curlpp | https://github.com/jpbarrette/curlpp | C++ wrapper around libcURL. | No numeric comparison against any named baseline reachable. A pistack.xyz blog post of C++ HTTP client libraries that may mention it was EGRESS_BLOCKED, so its contents are unknown. | w3-mine-e |

That is a self-comparison against an earlier version, not an outside baseline. PR #224 (open, 2026-08-21, by a contributor) reports before/after numbers for the library's own hot paths (FixedUnorderedMap find 1.77 to 1.52 ns, FixedVector insert+erase 23.3 to 16.2 ns, g++-13 -O3) but compares the library with its own earlier version, not with std::vector or std::map. Design goal is no dynamic allocation and constexpr use, not speed. | w1-mine-o |

The GitHub page (659 stars, LGPL-3.0 plus GPLv3 and commercial licensing, default branch main) and the README (readme.md) hold only qualitative claims ('Performance oriented', 'optimized'); the single number is that screenshots were captured on a 1080GTX at 1080p running at 60FPS, which compares nothing. It is built on bgfx, which supports Vulkan, Metal, DirectX 9-12, OpenGL and OpenGL ES, but no per-backend or OpenGL/DirectX timing is published. One WebSearch for Harfang versus Unity/Unreal performance found no benchmark mentioning Harfang. Guessed OpenGL/DirectX baseline does not apply (it is a layer over those APIs through bgfx). | w10-mine-i |
| Hashmaps | https://github.com/goossaert/hashmap | Implementation of open addressing hash table algorithms in C++. | No numeric comparison against a named external baseline reachable. README is two sentences; main.cc and the Makefile only run its five own variants (linear probing, Robin Hood with tombstones, Robin Hood with backward shift deletion, hopscotch bitmap and shadow) through loading, batch and ripple tests that measure when insertion fails and verify integrity, with no timings and no std::unordered_map. The author's blog posts that use this code (codecapsule.com Robin Hood hashing backward shift deletion, hopscotch hashing) are EGRESS_BLOCKED; a search snippet says they compare the variants by probe-distance metrics (DIB, DMB in bytes), not time against std::unordered_map. The 2-3x std::unordered_map snippet (4698 ms vs 1473 ms) seen in web search was not tied to goossaert's repo. | w1-mine-q |
| heatshrink | https://github.com/atomicobject/heatshrink | Data compression/decompression library for embedded and ... (lead text truncated; repo description: data compression/decompression library optimized for embedded and real-time systems) | Claims are about memory and bounded CPU per call (as low as 50 bytes, under 300 bytes for many cases), not speed against a named baseline. The README gives no numbers against zlib, LZO or lzjb; the repo's benchmark/ directory holds one shell script that sweeps heatshrink's own window and lookahead settings over the Canterbury corpus and reports its own ratios. The author's blog post (spin.atomicobject.com) is blocked and its search snippet describes only a ratio table across heatshrink settings. The one independent comparison found (BrianPugh/tamp README, enwik8 on an M3 MacBook Air) shows heatshrink slower than zlib at decompression (0.67 s vs 0.578 s), slower at compression without its index (27.40 s vs 3.65 s) and 4.42 s with the index, and bigger output than zlib on most Silesia files; its memory table gives heatshrink 1 << (windowBits + 1) bytes against zlib's (1 << (windowBits + 2)) + 7KB, a formula not a measurement, and memory is not a speed metric. | w9-mine-a |
| HEhub | https://github.com/primihub/HEhub | A library for homomorphic encryption and its applications. | No comparison against any named baseline. The README benchmark section (raw README plus the github.com repo page) gives only absolute timings for HEhub on an Intel i7-9750H with Clang 12.0.5 (NTT 7 us at N=1024 up to 330 us at N=32768; CKKS encode+encrypt 426 us at N=4096) and states 'The code for benchmark is still incomplete'. The bench/ sources (ntt_bm.cpp, ckks_bm.cpp, mod_arith_bm.cpp, benchmarks.cpp) are small; the first three are fully commented-out Catch2 code and benchmarks.cpp uses nanobench, and none references SEAL, OpenFHE, HElib or Lattigo. The four newest develop commits (2026-05-12) are titled '[opt] ...' (CKKS FFT, OpenMP, march=native/LTO) but their titles carry no numbers. A WebSearch for HEhub or primihub against SEAL and OpenFHE returned only third-party SEAL vs OpenFHE papers that do not include HEhub. | w7-mine-d |
| heman | https://github.com/prideout/heman | Tiny library of image utilities dealing with height maps, normal maps, distance fields and the like | No numeric comparison anywhere reachable. README (calls itself a toy project), docs/overview.rst, docs/index.rst and include/heman.h contain no timings, benchmark or baseline; the repo has src, include, test and docs but no benchmark directory. Only directional pointers to 'fast' published algorithms (Felzenszwalb distance transform, Bridson Poisson disk sampling, Sean Barrett's ambient occlusion sweeps) and an OpenMP build note. The guessed baseline 'standard image codec' is wrong: heman is a heightmap and normal-map utility, not a codec. | w1-mine-q |
| herebedragons | https://github.com/kosua20/herebedragons | A basic 3D scene implemented with various engines, frameworks or APIs | No numeric comparison against any named baseline reachable. It is a collection of 21 renderings of one scene (OpenGL, Vulkan, Metal, DX9/11/12, WebGL, WebGPU, Unity, Unreal, Godot, Cycles, a software renderer, consoles and others). The top README states the comparison is 'inherently biased' and gives no timings; all 21 per-API READMEs were fetched and searched for fps, ms, frame time, faster, slower and performance, and none has a frame time or FPS comparison (only 'running at 30 or 60 FPS' for the Dreamcast build and '25 or 50 FPS' for the PS2 build, each a mode of one build, not a comparison). Not a performance project; the guessed OpenGL/DirectX baseline gives no speed claim. | w10-mine-i |
| hiberlite | https://github.com/paulftw/hiberlite | C++ Object-relational mapping for sqlite3. | No numeric comparison anywhere reachable. A web search for C++ SQLite ORM benchmarks found only ODB's own results (7ms per 500 iterations on SQLite 3.7.5) and no table that includes hiberlite. | w6-mine-c |
| Hiredis | https://github.com/redis/hiredis | Minimalistic client library for Redis. | No numeric comparison of hiredis against a named baseline. Web results are about other things: a gist benchmarking the Node.js hiredis parser against a JavaScript parser (hiredis was the slower one at 44543 vs 46189 ops/sec for PING at 200 clients, Core 2 Duo Mac mini), snippets on the hiredis-py Python parser (a binding of this library, a wrapper) being up to 10x faster in parsing, and a snippet of C with hiredis at 340,000 commands/s vs Ruby redis-rb 150,000 whose source page was not opened. Hiredis is the reference client rather than a challenger, so no baseline it beats was found. | w6-mine-c |
| hobbes | https://github.com/Morgan-Stanley/hobbes | A language and an embedded JIT compiler from Morgan Stanley. | No numeric comparison against a recognizable baseline. README and docs (doc/en about, index, security, logging HLOG and hog pages) make design claims only (O(log r) match expressions, region allocation, 'efficient' native code, 'ultra-low latency') with no benchmark numbers; test/ has no perf or bench files; repo has no benchmark directory. Issues and PRs only carry self comparisons: issue #501 (match-table compilation about 5.8x slower with LLVM 16 than LLVM 8, a regression), PR #496 (about 7x faster match-table compiles versus the previous hobbes) and PR #578 (hobbes::cc construction about 20 percent faster). Web searches for talks or blogs with timing claims found none (habr.com article was EGRESS_BLOCKED, search excerpts show no numbers). Apache-2.0, 1.2k stars, active. | w12-mine-a |
| Horde3D | https://github.com/horde3d/Horde3D | A small 3D rendering and animation engine | No numeric comparison against any named baseline reachable. The GitHub page (1.6k stars, EPL-1.0) and README list features (occlusion culling, LOD, binary model format 'for maximum performance') with no benchmark or comparison; the only speed number is that Ninja builds up to two-three times faster than make, which is about compiling the project, not the engine. One WebSearch (Horde3D versus Ogre3D/Irrlicht fps) found only unrelated Irrlicht forum anecdotes, none with Horde3D numbers. horde3d.org and its wiki were not reachable. Guessed OpenGL/DirectX baseline does not apply (it is built on OpenGL). | w10-mine-i |
| HPX | https://github.com/STEllAR-GROUP/hpx/ | A general purpose C++ runtime system for parallel and distributed applications of any scale. | No reachable page with numbers where HPX beats a named baseline. Opened: README.rst (no figures, only 'hundreds of millions of threads' and scalability goals), docs/joss_paper/paper.md (no benchmark numbers), tests/performance/local (benchmark sources such as openmp_homogeneous_timed_task_spawn.cpp, openmp_parallel_region.cpp, stream.cpp, future_overhead.cpp but no stored results), PR 3183 (no numbers), issue 3137 (HPX start/stop 50 ms, HPX suspend/resume 500 us vs OpenMP parallel-for enter/exit 100 us on a dual-socket 18-core Xeon: OpenMP faster), the weilewei/parSTL repo (HPX, Kokkos, GNU, TBB parallel transform and sort harness, no results) and a gist on HPX benchmark standardisation (one hpx::min timing, no baseline). Search snippet only: tzcnt/runtime-benchmarks lists HPX about 161x slower than the best runtime (mean ratio) on a fork-join benchmark, and a June 2026 arXiv preprint 2606.11937 (Strack, Van Craen, Pfluger, tiled Cholesky with OpenMP and HPX, Cholesky-Bench, 128-core AMD Zen 2 node) reports HPX task overhead about 2 us vs about 7.6 us for GCC OpenMP, HPX 15-30% faster at the optimal tile size and async HPX tasks up to 26% faster than their OpenMP analogs; arxiv.org, ar5iv, alphaxiv, emergentmind and puma.ub.uni-stuttgart.de are not resolvable here and a guessed code repo (SC-SGS/Cholesky-Bench) returned 404, so those numbers could not be verified. Worth retrying with paper access: the Cholesky-Bench preprint (HPX vs GCC/LLVM OpenMP) and the Task Bench overheads paper (Wu et al., Charm++ and HPX vs MPI/OpenMP, seen only in a snippet). The HPX repo also appears under TheHPXProject/hpx. | w11-mine-h |

| KISS FFT | https://sourceforge.net/projects/kissfft/ | Simple fast Fourier transform library. | The SourceForge page has no benchmark numbers and no speed claim against FFTPACK or any other library (description: 'The FFT library to Keep It Simple, Stupid'; last update 2018-10-16; states that development moved to https://github.com/mborgerding/kissfft). Same project as the separate kissfft lead from the same list, so see that entry for the numbers that exist (all of which show it slower than FFTW, FFTPACK-derived pocketfft and others). The guessed baseline FFTPACK has no kissfft-wins row anywhere reachable except tiny sizes (n of 8 or less) in the pffft_benchmarks table. | w8-mine-c |
| kissfft | https://github.com/mborgerding/kissfft | A Fast Fourier Transform (FFT) library that tries to Keep it Simple, Stupid. | Nobody claims it is faster than a named baseline; every number found shows it slower. README BACKGROUND: 'FFT_BRANDX is roughly twice as fast as KISS FFT in default mode' (BRANDX unnamed, implied FFTW), 'DO NOT: use Kiss if you need the Fastest Fourier Transform in the World'; its PERFORMANCE section is one old timing (Athlon XP 2100+, gcc 2.96, float: 10000 1024-pt complex FFTs in 0.63 s) compared only with md5sum. Third-party numbers, flagged not-faster if the orchestrator wants a record: project-gemmi/benchmarking-fft (i7-5600U, GCC 8 -O3, float, 1D complex-to-complex n=512: kissfft 6553 ns vs fftw3 measured 588 ns, mufft 719 ns, pffft 1255 ns, and with SIMD disabled pocketfft 4009 ns, meow_fft 4342 ns; 3D 416x256x416: kissfft 4235 ms vs fftw3 measured 727 ms, pocketfft 939 ms); hayguen/pffft_benchmarks, i7-8550U gcc 9.3.0 single-precision complex n=1024: Kiss 5831 MFLOPS vs FFTPack 8579, FFTW F(auto) 55947 (Kiss beats FFTPack only at n of 8 or less: n=2 1154 vs 497, n=4 3424 vs 1322, n=8 2436 vs 1848 MFLOPS, and that table uses hayguen's fork of kissfft). Pages opened: https://github.com/mborgerding/kissfft, https://github.com/project-gemmi/benchmarking-fft, https://github.com/hayguen/pffft_benchmarks. Its value is size and simplicity (core about 500 lines, fixed-point support), not speed. | w8-mine-c |

Web search snippets only say the OOPSLA 2012 paper (Hayden et al.) reports 'essentially no performance overhead' and fast update times for Redis, Memcached and others; no figures and no speed-versus-restart baseline were obtainable. Its efficiency claim is low overhead relative to the unmodified program, not a speedup over a faster baseline. Retry where the UMD paper or the project site is reachable (journal version: hayden14kitsune-journal). | w11-mine-b |
| Kratos | https://github.com/KratosMultiphysics/Kratos | framework for building parallel, multi-disciplinary simulation software, aiming at modularity, extensibility, and high performance. | No numeric comparison against any named baseline reachable. The guessed category blas-lapack and baselines ODE or Bullet are wrong: Kratos is a finite-element and multiphysics framework (fluid, structure, DEM) whose natural baselines would be FEniCS, deal.II or MOOSE; its published papers were not reachable (arXiv and journal sites blocked). | w8-mine-c |

The old README performance section, per a search snippet, shows Kvrocks-only redis-benchmark charts (Xeon E5-2650 v4, 48 cores) with no Redis, Pika or SSDB columns. Issue #322 'any benchmark against redis?': the maintainer says a direct comparison makes little sense and Kvrocks would 'likely' beat Redis on throughput for many commands, with no numbers; the only numbers are a user's redis-benchmark runs on DigitalOcean in which Kvrocks is not faster than KeyDB (4 vCPU shared, 8 GB NVMe, 10 threads: SET 34,352 vs 59,066 req/s, GET 56,786 vs 56,689 req/s) and no Redis run. Issue #99 reports Kvrocks writes slower than ledis, ssdb or redix, no numbers. Web searches found no third-party Redis, Pika or SSDB comparison. | LAME | https://lame.sourceforge.io/using.php | LAME is a high quality MPEG Audio Layer III (MP3) encoder. | No numeric speed comparison in LAME's favor against any named baseline reachable. lame.sourceforge.io was not reachable (getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND); the rbrito/lame GitHub mirror's README, USAGE, doc/html/index.html and 900 KB ChangeLog were read: the ChangeLog has only self-comparisons of its own versions (for example 'make VBR/ABR 1% faster', 'speed up of bin_search_stepsize ... 20% faster') and no other encoder is timed. Three WebSearches found: the 3.96/3.97 release notes (search snippet only) claim only 'Encodes faster than real time on a PII 266 at highest quality mode' (no named baseline) and quality parity with FhG; the Hydrogenaudio 128 kbps listening test (October 2008, snippet only, page blocked) says Fraunhofer and Helix 'outperform LAME in terms of encoding speed' (Helix at 90x), i.e. the evidence runs against LAME; a Phoronix/OpenBenchmarking LAME 3.100 (snippet only) WAV-to-MP3 test reports only LAME's own run times (Min 1 / Avg 1.73 / Max 12 minutes across systems). The project goal is quality, not speed; no recognizable baseline is beaten with numbers. | w10-mine-i |

Reachable evidence (github.com/gpg/libgcrypt NEWS, raw file) lists only speedups of libgcrypt over its own earlier releases (for example AES-XTS 6 times faster, SHA-1/SHA-256 4.0/3.7 times faster with Intel SHA extensions, SHA-512 4.5 times faster on Ryzen 7). Reachable jkivilin/bench-slopes README (OpenSSL, Nettle, Botan, Crypto++, libgcrypt) prints example outputs from different machines and library versions, not a head-to-head. Two candidate comparisons exist but their pages are EGRESS_BLOCKED, so only WebSearch summaries were seen and nothing was verified: (1) gnupg.org/blog/20131215-gcrypt-bench.html 'Speedups in Libgcrypt 1.6' (summary: libgcrypt 1.6 reaches top speed of all compared implementations for SHA-1, SHA-256/512 improved, AES throughput well above 1.5 and considerably above Nettle); (2) a 2018 nettle-bugs mailing-list message on lists.lysator.liu.se (summary: AES-128-CBC on a Core i7-6820HQ with Fedora 26 packages: nettle-3.3 850 MB/s, libgcrypt-1.7.8 1172 MB/s, gnutls-3.5.14 1230 MB/s, openssl-1.1.0f 1153 MB/s; libgcrypt only 1.7% above OpenSSL). Retry both from an environment that can open them. Suggested category aead-and-ciphers (crypto-and-hashing), not concurrency-queues-locks; LGPL-2.1+. | w1-mine-t |
| Libgcrypt | https://github.com/gpg/libgcrypt | A general purpose cryptographic library originally based on code from GnuPG. | No reachable numeric comparison against an external baseline. NEWS (raw.githubusercontent.com/gpg/libgcrypt/HEAD/NEWS) lists only speedups over its own earlier releases (AES-XTS 6 times faster with AES-NI, SHA-1/SHA-256 4.0/3.7 times faster with Intel SHA extensions, SHA-512 4.5 times faster on Ryzen 7, VAES/AVX512 about 2 times OCB on AMD Zen5). The jkivilin/bench-slopes README (libgcrypt, OpenSSL, Nettle, Botan, Crypto++ drivers) only prints example outputs from different machines and library versions, not a head-to-head, and the scc-tw/cryptobench-cpp suite (result.json) has no libgcrypt column. Candidate comparisons all sit on hosts that do not resolve here (ENOTFOUND for gnupg.org, lists.gnupg.org, lists.lysator.liu.se, panthema.net); only WebSearch summaries were seen and nothing was verified: the 2013 'Speedups in Libgcrypt 1.6' blog on gnupg.org (says libgcrypt had long trailed OpenSSL and Nettle was faster in places), a 2017 nettle-bugs thread (AES-128-CBC with AES-NI on an i7-6820HQ: nettle 850, libgcrypt 1172, gnutls 1230, openssl 1153 MB/s, so libgcrypt about 1.7 percent above OpenSSL), and the 2008 panthema.net crypto speedtest (libgcrypt reported much slower than Botan, Crypto++, LibTomCrypt). Retry from an environment that can open them. Suggested category aead-and-ciphers; LGPL-2.1-or-later. | w7-mine-d |
| libgd | https://github.com/libgd/libgd | Library for the dynamic creation of images by programmers (awesome-cpp Image Processing; awesome-c Graphics) | No project speed claim; README, CHANGELOG and docs have no benchmark text. The only numbers are third-party and mostly unfavourable: github.com/libvips/libvips/wiki/Speed-and-memory-use (libvips author's vips-bench, AMD Ryzen Threadripper PRO 3955WX, Ubuntu 25.04, run 12 Aug 2025, JPEG crop+shrink+sharpen of a 10000x10000 image, best of five) lists jpg-gd (libgd 2.3.3) at 5.47 s, 761.68 MB peak, 6.67x slower than libvips (vips-c 8.18, 0.57 s). libgd is slower than convert 4.44 s, magick 4.41 s, gm 2.05 s, pillow-simd 1.51 s, OpenCV 3.15 s and imlib2 3.43 s, and faster only than pnm (netpbm) 6.06 s, PHP imagick 10.53 s, gmic 11.63 s and octave 35.1 s; the page notes libgd could not read TIFF so JPEG input was used. That is a non-claim by a rival project, so no record was written; a later pass may add it as a keep-and-document entry if wanted. MIT. Suggested category image-codecs or rasterizers-2d (media-and-graphics), not small-buffer-and-strings. | w1-mine-t |
| libgeohash | https://github.com/simplegeo/libgeohash | Pure C implementation of the Geohash algorithm. (awesome-c, Utilities) | No numeric comparison. Repo (BSD-3-Clause, 111 stars, 21 commits, authored by Derek Smith) has only a geohash_test.c correctness test and no README benchmark. raw.githubusercontent.com README.md returned 404. A WebSearch for geohash benchmarks surfaced a Python package also named libgeohash and .NET geohash libraries (NetGeohash and others), which are different projects. Suggested category geometry-spatial (numerics-and-science). | w1-mine-t |
| libgiconv | https://gnu.org/software/libiconv/ | Text conversion library. | No numeric comparison reachable. GNU libiconv (the project behind this lead) is a portability library: its README (read via the gnu-mirror-unofficial/libiconv GitHub mirror) lists supported encodings and makes no speed claim, and its NEWS has no performance numbers. gnu.org is blocked here. Web searches for libiconv versus glibc iconv found only unrelated rows (uclibc-ng's own UTF-8 to wchar rows, a Crystal 'charconv' reimplementation beating the system iconv); libiconv is at most a baseline in other projects' tables, never the faster side. | w1-mine-u |
| libharu | https://github.com/libharu/libharu | A free, cross platform, open-sourced software library for generating PDF. | Only speed claim is in README: 'A new-version library is about 4-15 times faster than a previous version' (libHaru 1.x C++ rewritten in C for 2.x), a self-comparison against an unnamed old version with no workload, hardware or methodology. No comparison against poppler, PDFium, PoDoFo or any other PDF library in README, CHANGES, issues (searched performance/faster/benchmark: only #357 and #95 appear) or a 2026 pistack.xyz blog survey (qualitative only). | w10-mine-j |
| libhl | https://github.com/xant/libhl | Library implementing a thread-safe API to manage a range of data | No benchmarks anywhere reachable. README has no performance claim beyond the About text 'Simple and fast C library'; test/ holds functional tests only (hashtable_test.c etc.); a WebSearch for comparisons against uthash, GLib or Concurrency Kit found none. Directional claim without numbers. | w11-mine-j |
| libhttp | https://github.com/lammertb/libhttp | A cross-platform HTTP and HTTPS library in C/C++. | No numeric comparison anywhere reachable. README (MIT, about 1,000 stars, fork of Civetweb/Mongoose, not archived) has feature lists and no benchmark or speed claim; issue #42 'Benchmarks?' (2017, closed) asks for benchmarks and has no reply with numbers; issue #41 'Why?' is unanswered; the project site libhttp.org is not reachable here. Web searches found no third-party shootout that includes it, and the framework shootouts opened (nanoant/WebFrameworkBenchmark, liu-rui/webframeworkbenchmark) list libmicrohttpd, Kore and Onion but not libhttp or Civetweb. | w5-mine-k |
| libhttpd | https://www.hughes.com.au/products/libhttpd/ | Library to add basic web server capabilities to an application (Hughes Technologies). | Directional only. The project pages (hughes.com.au, hughestech.com.au) are EGRESS_BLOCKED here; search excerpts say only that the library has 'very low overheads' with no numbers or baseline, and no third-party benchmark naming libhttpd was found. | w5-mine-k |
| libhttpserver | https://github.com/etr/libhttpserver | C++ library for creating an embedded Rest HTTP server (and more). | No numeric comparison reachable. README (LGPL-2.1, 953 stars, wrapper over libmicrohttpd) calls itself high-performance and lists tuning options (turbo, TCP_FASTOPEN, suppress_date_header) and three examples/benchmark_*.cpp micro-benchmark servers, but no result numbers; ChangeLog 0.17.5 says 'saving ~15% on average per request' from URL normalisation (self-comparison), PR #365 lists optimisations with no numbers, issue #169 is a bug report (26,716 req/s without httperf rate limiting) with no baseline, and DR-005 only says allocation savings matter above 10k resp/s. TechEmpower FrameworkBenchmarks has a frameworks/C++/libhttpserver plaintext entry (archived 2026-03-24), but techempower.com results are blocked here, so no number could be read; lead for a later pass with wider network access. | w5-mine-k |
| libhydrogen | https://github.com/jedisct1/libhydrogen | A lightweight, secure, easy-to-use crypto library suitable for constrained environments. | No speed comparison anywhere reachable. README and the 15-page wiki give design goals (Gimli plus Curve25519 only, zero heap allocation, stack median 32 bytes and max 128 bytes) and explicitly list 'replacing libsodium' as a non-goal; no benchmark directory in the repo root listing; one WebSearch for libhydrogen versus libsodium speed returned nothing. 785 stars, ISC, active. | w7-mine-a |
| libigl | https://github.com/libigl/libigl | Simple C++ geometry processing library (awesome-cpp, Graphics; licence tag MPL2) | README and docs index carry no speed claim. Only numbers found: libigl PR #1895 (Sep 2021, maintainer, 56-core Mac Pro) says ~14x faster remesh_self_intersections and ~7x faster mesh_boolean, but against libigl's own previous mutex-heavy code, not another library, with no inputs or methodology. The 6.8x-over-CGAL figure for the SIGGRAPH 2016 Mesh Arrangements method appears only in a secondhand OpenSCAD mailing-list summary (lists.openscad.org, blocked here) and the paper itself was unreachable. Third-party papers (arXiv 2205.14151 'almost 25x faster than libigl') show libigl as the slower baseline. Lead: Zhou et al. SIGGRAPH 2016 Mesh Arrangements paper tables (mesh booleans vs CGAL Nef, Carve) if paper access opens; category would be geometry/meshing, not color-and-resampling. | w10-mine-k |
| libimagequant | https://github.com/ImageOptim/libimagequant | Small, portable library for high-quality conversion of RGBA images to 8-bit indexed colour images. | No page with numbers reachable. Opened the README (no speed text), CHANGELOG (self-comparisons only: 'less precise remapping improving speed by 25% in higher speed settings', 'reduced memory usage by further 30%') and the pngquant README (--speed N trade-off, no baseline). pngquant.org, imagemagick.org and jqmagick.imagemagick.org are blocked (EGRESS_BLOCKED). The only baseline claim is a search snippet, 'fast-and-rough speed setting 2.5 to 6 times faster than ImageMagick's current algorithm', from the author's legacy ImageMagick forum thread; it is already catalogued as the wave 10 entry 'pngquant / libimagequant' (https://github.com/kornelski/pngquant, tier C, snippet only), so no second record was written. The only newer numbers found are okooo5km/pngoptim's README (pngoptim 1.69x faster than pngquant), where libimagequant's tool is the slower baseline. | w1-mine-u |
| libjson-rpc-cpp | https://github.com/cinemast/libjson-rpc-cpp | JSON-RPC framework for C++ servers and clients. | No numeric comparison anywhere reachable. README (MIT, 986 stars) is about features, the stub generator and JSON-RPC 2.0 compatibility with no benchmark or speed claim; the only performance-related issues (#170 poll/epoll by default, #96 async client) carry no numbers against another library; web search found no benchmark against gRPC or any other RPC framework. | w5-mine-k |
| liblfds | https://liblfds.org/ | Portable lock-free data structure library. Public domain (more exactly, whatever license you want). | Benchmark results exist (libbenchmark compares liblfds against locking versions of the same structures; blog posts liblfds.org/slblog/2016-02 to 2016-05, and a btree benchmark said to show lock-free winning by 5x to 10x) but liblfds.org and www.liblfds.org are EGRESS_BLOCKED, and the github.com/liblfds mirrors (liblfds, liblfds7.1.1; README says development moved to liblfds.org self-hosted git) hold no benchmark results. Only WebSearch summaries were seen, which also say pthread mutex won one queue benchmark and pthread spinlock won at maximum thread count, so the claim is mixed; no named baseline, hardware or flags could be verified. Retry where liblfds.org is reachable (release 7.1.1 libbenchmark results). | w11-mine-b |
| libmicrohttpd | https://gnu.org/software/libmicrohttpd/ | Small library that makes it easy to run an HTTP server as part of another application. | No numeric comparison against a named incumbent. Opened the unofficial GitHub mirror Karlson2k/libmicrohttpd (README: binary about 30-40k when size-optimised, no benchmark data). gnu.org and git.gnunet.org were blocked by egress policy. A WebSearch snippet of the manual says an epoll thread pool with 4 threads exceeded 45,000 connections per second on loopback with Apache Benchmark, but that compares MHD's own select against epoll, not another server. No shootout found that includes it (the C++ REST framework benchmark compares cpprestsdk, restbed, pistache and PHP only). | w5-mine-l |
| libmongoc | https://github.com/mongodb/mongo-c-driver | High-performance client library for MongoDB. | The repo README makes no speed comparison. The companion repo mongodb/mongo-c-driver-performance only implements MongoDB's standard driver benchmark suite and prints bytes per second for libbson/libmongoc tests with no baseline column; searches for a cross-driver comparison (Python, Go, Java drivers) found only Jira tickets and forum threads without published numbers. | w6-mine-a |
| libmpv (mpv) | https://github.com/mpv-player/mpv | Music-playing library, GPL-2.0-or-later (awesome-c, Multimedia; https://mpv.io) | No numbers in README or in DOCS/man/options.rst (only unquantified 'big speedup' for lavc skip options and a note that zimg may be slower than libswscale). No benchmark repo. The only numeric comparison found is a single user report in VLC issue 25795 (VLC about 6% CPU, mpv under 2%, VLC 3.0.12) seen via a search snippet; code.videolan.org is blocked (getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND), so the page could not be opened. Other web claims (10-20% less CPU than VLC at 4K) are from an unsourced blog post. Lead: VLC issue 25795 if videolan.org becomes reachable; it would be tier C anecdote at best. | w10-mine-k |
| libnih | https://github.com/keybuk/libnih | Lightweight library of functions and structures. | No numeric comparison anywhere reachable. README states goals (not reimplementing the C library, simple consistent API, D-Bus and expat dependencies) with no performance text; no benchmark directory is documented; the project is a utility/D-Bus helper library, not a speed-oriented replacement. | w1-mine-u |
| libnl | https://www.infradead.org/~tgr/libnl/ | Collection of libraries providing APIs to the Linux kernel netlink protocol interfaces. | Netlink library, not an HTTP server; guessed category was wrong. github.com/thom311/libnl page (LGPL-2.1, 490 stars) and raw ChangeLog have no benchmark, speed or performance numbers. A search for libnl versus libmnl found only qualitative statements that libmnl is more minimal, no numbers. | w5-mine-l |
| libonion | https://github.com/davidmoreno/onion | HTTP server library, designed to be easy to | Only number is the README line that pool mode with epoll served 'up to 30k web-requests' on an Intel Core 2 Duo T6500 2.10GHz, an absolute figure with no baseline. | liboqs | https://github.com/open-quantum-safe/liboqs | Library for quantum-resistant cryptographicl algorithms. | No numeric comparison against an outside baseline reachable. README and docs/ (algorithms/, FUZZING.md, PROCEDURES.md) have no benchmark numbers, only the speed_kem, speed_sig and speed_sig_stfl programs; the open-quantum-safe/profiling benchmark repo is archived (June 2025) and its README gives no numbers; openquantumsafe.org is blocked. Search results with numbers are self-comparisons (liboqs AVX-512 versus AVX2, 1.30x to 1.64x, in an Intel builders paper; OQS_DIST_BUILD on versus off) or third-party arXiv papers (blocked) using liboqs as one column. Category guess (concurrency-queues-locks) is wrong; it is a post-quantum crypto library aggregating PQClean and pqcrystals code. | w1-mine-u |
| libpcap | https://github.com/the-tcpdump-group/libpcap | API provides to various kernel packet capture mechanism. Raw README.md, CHANGES and doc/README.linux contain no benchmark or speedup numbers (grep for faster, speed, perform, throughput, benchmark found only timestamp and link-speed hits). | w5-mine-l |
| libpng | https://github.com/pnggroup/libpng | Official PNG reference library. | libpng is the incumbent baseline in other projects' tables, not a challenger, so no outside baseline exists. Its own CHANGES file has only self-comparisons: 1.6.36 'Optimized png_do_expand_palette for ARM processors. Improved performance by around 10-22% on a recent ARM Chromebook' (versus earlier libpng), and unquantified SIMD notes (SSE2, NEON, VSX, LoongArch LSX, RISC-V RVV 'improved the performance'); a search snippet gives 20-25% NEON decode gain from a Linaro blueprint (self-comparison, page not opened). README, ANNOUNCE.md, TODO.md and contrib/arm-neon/README carry no numbers; libpng.org is blocked. | w1-mine-u |
| libpqxx | https://github.com/jtv/libpqxx | The official C++ client API for PostgreSQL. | Directional claims only, no numbers. NEWS for 7.8.0 and the GitHub 7.8.0 release say 'Streaming large data sets now benchmarks faster than similar C/libpq code!' with no figures; include/pqxx/doc/streams.md gives only qualitative toy-benchmark notes (a hundred or a thousand one-integer rows: streaming slower than exec; ten thousand rows: streaming faster; four-column rows: 'considerably faster' at a thousand rows) with no timings; tools/benchmark.cxx (added in 8.0.2) compares raw libpq, exec() and stream() but the repo publishes no results; issue searches found no timing comparison against libpq (#770 is about iterator copy cost only). The guessed SQLite baseline is wrong: the only relevant baseline is raw libpq, and a wrapper is not otherwise claimed to be faster. Repo is BSD-3-Clause, 1.4k stars. | w6-mine-d |
| libquic | https://github.com/devsisters/libquic | A QUIC protocol library extracted from Chromium's QUIC Implementation. | github.com/devsisters/libquic (BSD-3-Clause, 1.8k stars) README has no benchmark numbers and no comparison with nghttp3 or any other QUIC stack; it only says QUIC is experimental and aims to reduce latency versus TCP. Any published QUIC-versus-TCP numbers belong to Google's Chromium QUIC, not this extraction. | w5-mine-l |
| libquickmail | http://sourceforge.net/projects/libquickmail/ | Library intended to give developers a way to send e-mail (with attachments) from C/C++ programs. | Email-sending library (GPL-3.0, last release 0.1.30 on 2023-05-04). The SourceForge project page has no benchmark or comparison with libcurl SMTP or any other mail library. | w5-mine-l |
| librb | https://github.com/mlyszczek/librb | C implementation of ring (circular) buffer, with full thread awareness that allows for concurrent read/write and can automatically grow in size when needed. | No numeric comparison reachable. readme.rst, www/rb_overview.7.rst and the repo tree (rb.c, rb.h.in, tests.c, examples/, man/, www/; no bench directory) contain only 'fast' as an adjective and test-count figures (68,874 tests); librb.bofc.pl is blocked (EGRESS_BLOCKED). No third-party shootout including librb was found. | w1-mine-u |
| librdkafka | https://github.com/edenhill/librdkafka | Apache Kafka client library for C and C++. | Only claim is README text 'current figures exceed 1 million msgs/second for the producer and 3 million msgs/second for the consumer': absolute figures, no named baseline, no hardware, no flags. Raw README.md, INTRODUCTION.md, the wiki index and the Performance wiki page have no comparison against the Java client or any other client. The confluent-kafka-python README only says it significantly outperforms pure Python clients, with no numbers (the 5-10x figure seen via WebSearch comes from a content-farm snippet about the Python binding, not librdkafka itself). Third-party Go shootouts (franz-go claims) show librdkafka-based confluent-kafka-go slower, not faster. | w5-mine-l |
| LibreSSL | https://github.com/libressl/portable | A free version of the SSL/TLS protocol forked from OpenSSL in 2014. | No numeric claim of being faster than OpenSSL. The README and ChangeLog (raw.githubusercontent.com/libressl/portable/HEAD) mention performance only qualitatively ('Significantly improved Montgomery multiplication performance', 'Improve SHA-3 performance by using an unrolled and interleaved algorithm', new SHA-1/SHA-256/SHA-512 amd64 assembly using SHA-NI) with no figures or baseline. KazKobara/plot-openssl-speed (github.com README) says LibreSSL 2.9.1 and newer shows aes-128/256-gcm 'by far faster' and 'similar results as OpenSSL 1', but states no numbers and its figures are PNGs stored as git-lfs pointers (unreachable), and the sentence does not say which library or cipher it is compared with. Where numbers surfaced they favour OpenSSL: WebSearch snippets only, not opened, of a FreeBSD forum post (AES-128-CBC 8 KB about 413 MB/s OpenSSL 1.0.2k vs about 22 MB/s LibreSSL 2.6.4 without AES-NI) and a Phoronix forum comment (OpenSSL 1.1.1 ECDSA up to 380 percent faster than LibreSSL 2.7). The HAProxy 'State of SSL Stacks' paper (OpenSSL 3.x vs LibreSSL, WolfSSL, AWS-LC) and the Phoronix LibreSSL 4.1 article could not be opened (ENOTFOUND for cdn.haproxy.com, www.haproxy.com, www.phoronix.com); retry them for handshake and SHA-NI numbers. | w7-mine-d |

LGPL-3.0-or-later. | github.com/risoflora/libsagui (94 stars) README only says it is 'so fast, compact and useful' because it builds on GNU libmicrohttpd; no benchmark, req/s figure or comparison with another server. | libschrift | https://github.com/tomolt/libschrift | A lightweight TrueType font rendering library | No numeric comparison against any other library.
