| libsequence | https://github.com/molpopgen/libsequence | A C++ library for representing and analyzing population genetics data. | No numeric comparison against any other library. README has no benchmarks; there is no benchmark directory. REVISION_HISTORY.md and ChangeLog only record speedups against its own earlier versions (for example a 10-fold speed-up of PolySNP statistics and a 6-fold speed-up of operator>>), which are not a recognizable baseline. A 2005 Bioperl mailing-list remark that libsequence statistics would be roughly 'a bazillion' times faster than Bioperl is anecdotal with no numbers (WebSearch snippet only). Repo is GPL-3.0 and archived by its owner on 2023-10-12. | w8-mine-j |
| Libserial | https://github.com/crayzeewulf/libserial | Serial Port Programming in C++. | README and WebSearch show no performance claim or numbers; it is an object-oriented wrapper over POSIX serial I/O with no named baseline compared (WebSearch for benchmarks against termios or Boost.Asio serial found none). | w5-mine-m |
| libsndfile | https://github.com/libsndfile/libsndfile | Library for reading and writing sound files (awesome-c, Multimedia; listed URL http://www.mega-nerd.com/libsndfile/, not opened, host not reachable here) | README, NEWS.OLD and CHANGELOG.md carry no speed claim or numbers; 1.0.31 adds an SSE2 psf_lrintf() with no benchmark. Only indirect numbers: faroit/python_audio_loading_benchmark times PySoundFile (libsndfile binding) against scipy, pydub, audioread-FFmpeg, aubio, librosa, but results are chart images with no numeric table or CPU model, the repo's pickled result tables disagree with the PNG charts, FFmpeg is spawned as a subprocess there, and scipy.io.wavfile beats soundfile on WAV. Not a libsndfile-versus-baseline claim. Lead: that benchmark's results/ pickles if someone reruns it with a stated CPU. | w10-mine-k |
| libsodium | https://github.com/jedisct1/libsodium | Modern and easy-to-use crypto library. | No number against a named baseline anywhere reachable. README.markdown has no benchmarks; libsodium-doc says only that primitives are 'faster across-the-board than most implementations', that crypto_generichash (BLAKE2b) is 'faster than MD5 and SHA-1', and that ChaCha20 is 'around three times as fast' as AES on platforms without AES hardware (algorithm-level, no methodology). ChangeLog speedups (SHA-2 20% faster, AVX2 ChaCha20 2x SSE2) compare libsodium against its own earlier versions. download.libsodium.org and lib25519.cr.yp.to (cycle tables against OpenSSL) were blocked. Lead: lib25519 speed tables and eBACS/SUPERCOP for a libsodium-versus-OpenSSL row once those hosts are reachable. | w1-mine-v |
| libsoundio | https://github.com/andrewrk/libsoundio | Library for cross-platform, real-time audio input and output (awesome-c, Multimedia; listed URL http://libsound.io, not opened) | README says only that the design leans toward performance over API convenience and that raw devices give best performance; no numbers, no PortAudio or other comparison, no benchmark in repo (dirs: cmake, doc, example, soundio, src, test; the test 'latency' program is a beep alignment check). Web searches found no measured latency or CPU comparison with PortAudio or others, only unsourced prose and one user anecdote. | w10-mine-k |
| libsvm | https://github.com/cjlin1/libsvm | A simple, easy-to-use, efficient library for Support Vector Machines. | The repo has no benchmark numbers: README (file name README, not README.md) and FAQ.html contain no comparison with any other library; the only speed statements are internal (sparse format makes kernel evaluation slower so training can take 'at least twice or three times' as long as dense; the Java port is 'still slower' than C++). The guessed baselines tensorflow and pytorch are wrong: LIBSVM is a kernel-SVM solver and is itself the usual baseline that ThunderSVM, liquidSVM and other SVM tools compare against (scikit-learn's SVC wraps it). The project homepage www.csie.ntu.edu.tw/~cjlin/libsvm and PMC were EGRESS_BLOCKED. A WebSearch result summary mentioned a liquidSVM paper table (arXiv 1702.06899, not opened, unverified) with COD-RNA n=1000 training times LIBSVM 34.5 s vs SVMlight 665.6 s, and another snippet said SVMlight is faster on sparse data; treat as a lead for an environment with arXiv access, not as evidence. | w8-mine-c |
| libtomcrypt | https://github.com/libtom/libtomcrypt | Fairly comprehensive, modular and portable. | Only a directional claim: doc/crypt.tex says cycles-per-byte timings are 'comparable (and often favourable) to other libraries such as OpenSSL and Crypto++', with no numbers, versions or hardware. The changes file lists only self-versus-self speedups (Shamir's trick 1.34x to 1.40x faster ECC verification, GCM table creation 12.7x, ECC projective points 3.21x to 4.53x). No third-party shootout including libtomcrypt was reachable (libtom.net blocked; FFmpeg's crypto_bench includes it but results not found). | w1-mine-v |
| LibTomMath | https://github.com/libtom/libtommath | Portable, number-theoretic, multiple-precision integer library. | No claim of being faster than anything. The author's manual (doc/bn.tex in github.com/libtom/libtommath, section 'Purpose of LibTomMath') states the library was not written with bleeding-edge performance in mind, that it is slower than GnuMP and than GnuPG v1.2.3's math, and that on x86 the difference is normally a factor of two for modular exponentiation (v0.28-0.35 era numbers, no methodology). The README has no benchmarks. Third-party comparisons found (libzahl STATUS/commit log) compare libzahl with GMP and TomsFastMath, not LibTomMath as the winner; TomsFastMath is the fork that claims to be faster than LibTomMath. | w8-mine-j |
| LibTomPoly | https://github.com/libtom/libtompoly | Polynomial-related maths library. Public domain. | README (raw.githubusercontent.com/libtom/libtompoly/HEAD/README.md) and the repo page have no mention of speed, benchmarks or timing; it is a small polynomial-basis arithmetic layer on LibTomMath (9 commits on develop, 40 stars). No comparison against any named baseline found. | w8-mine-j |
| libtorrent | https://github.com/arvidn/libtorrent | An efficient feature complete C++ bittorrent implementation. | README, docs/features.rst, docs/tuning.rst and the RC_2_1 ChangeLog describe efficiency design (single-copy disk buffers, rarest-first piece picker, many 'improve performance' notes) but give no numbers against another BitTorrent implementation or library; web search for comparisons with libtransmission, rtorrent or aria2 found none. | w5-mine-c |
| libtrading | https://github.com/libtrading/libtrading | Implementation of network protocols for communicating with exchanges, dark pools and other trading venues. Supports FIX, FIX/FAST and many proprietary protocols. | README gives own latency numbers (FIX round trip 6 us, FAST 13 us, fix_client avg 16.8 us on a Haswell 3.60 GHz) but compares against no other FIX engine; wiki home has no benchmark; libtrading.org is egress-blocked and WebSearch found no QuickFIX comparison. Absolute numbers without a named baseline do not qualify. | w5-mine-m |
| libU | https://github.com/koanlogic/libu | Small library of basic utilities, including memory allocation, string manipulation and logging. | No performance claims or benchmark numbers in the repository page or its doc directory (doxygen and man pages only); koanlogic.com blocked. Last commit 2020-12-21 (a typo-fix merge), 461 stars, BSD licence. | w1-mine-v |
| libunistring | https://gnu.org/software/libunistring/ | Library for manipulating Unicode. | No benchmark where libunistring is the faster library. gnu.org was blocked. The only snippets found (WebSearch, pages not opened) say its speed is similar to utf8proc and that ICU normalisation is faster than libunistring and utf8proc; the utf8proc issue #12 that was opened only requests benchmarks and holds no numbers. | w1-mine-v |
| liburcu | https://github.com/urcu/userspace-rcu | Data synchronization library, which scales linearly with the number of cores. | Repository (README, doc/rcu-api.md, doc/cds-api.md, ChangeLog) carries no benchmark results, only the benchmark programs in tests/benchmark (test_rwlock.c, test_urcu.c, test_urcu_hash.c and others) and the make short_bench and long_bench targets. The real evidence is the paper 'User-Level Implementations of Read-Copy Update' (Desnoyers, McKenney, Stern, Dagenais, Walpole, IEEE TPDS 23(2), 2012), whose abstract says RCU read-side primitives are an order of magnitude faster than uncontended locking, but efficios.com, dorsal.polymtl.ca, lttng.org, lwn.net and liburcu.org were all blocked and no GitHub copy was found (snippet only). Lead: retry paper access; likely tier B with reader-scalability numbers against pthread rwlock. | w1-mine-v |
| libutp | https://github.com/bittorrent/libutp | uTorrent Transport Protocol library. | README has no numbers; uTP is a delay-minimizing LEDBAT transport, not claimed faster than TCP; WebSearch found no libutp benchmark against TCP (the one blog test surfaced covers a different uTP implementation and was egress-blocked). | w5-mine-m |
| libuvc | https://github.com/libuvc/libuvc | A cross-platform library for USB video devices. | No numeric comparison anywhere reachable. The GitHub repo page (1.2k stars) and README carry no benchmark or performance claim (only the defaults LIBUVC_PACKETS_PER_TRANSFER_MAX 32 and LIBUVC_NUM_TRANSFER_BUFS 100); issue titles #111 'Avoid unnecessary memcpy' and #74 'asynchronous/callback vs synchronous/polling' name no baseline and no numbers. One WebSearch for libuvc vs V4L2 latency/fps found only unrelated Jetson/V4L2 forum numbers, nothing for libuvc. It is a USB video-class camera access library, not a codec, so 'platform video codec' is not a real baseline. | w10-mine-l |
| libVLC | https://github.com/videolan/vlc | Complete multimedia library for audio and video | No numeric claim that libVLC beats a named baseline. github.com/videolan/vlc (19.9k stars, GPL-2.0-or-later with LGPL-2.1-or-later libVLC) and its README make no performance comparison; wiki.videolan.org was unreachable (ENOTFOUND). One WebSearch found only a VLC forum anecdote where ffmpeg CLI decode ran faster than VLC fast-playback (ffmpeg about 84x realtime 480p vs VLC about 12x), i.e. the opposite direction and not a library-level benchmark. libVLC uses libavcodec for most decoding. | w10-mine-l |
| libvldmail | https://github.com/dertuxmalwieder/libvldmail | Your friendly email validation library. No external dependencies (not even regexps). | No numeric comparison anywhere reachable. README.md (MIT-0, 69 stars, upstream moved to Fossil with nightly Git mirrors) and the CHANGES file were read and grepped for fast, speed, bench, perf, slow and regex: the only regex mention is the design choice 'no regex, no ICU, no other overhead', stated as a feature with no timing. The repo root holds test.c, autotest-cases and the man page, with no benchmark source. Web search for libvldmail benchmark or speed found only unrelated validators (a dev.to regex-free validator with its own 13.7 percent figure, htmlmail) and no result that includes libvldmail. | w1-mine-w |
| LibVNCServer | https://github.com/LibVNC/libvncserver | Cross-platform libraries to implement VNC server and/or client. | README only has a 'good performance on slow links' tuning section with no numbers; WebSearch found no benchmark against TigerVNC or other VNC servers. Not an HTTP server either, so the guessed category does not apply. | w5-mine-m |
| libvpx | https://github.com/webmproject/libvpx | VP8/VP9 Codec SDK. | No reachable numeric claim that libvpx is faster than a named baseline. The GitHub mirror (975 stars, BSD-3-Clause) README and page have no benchmark. Searches only surface evidence that libvpx is slower than the incumbent: Ronald Bultje's 2014 ffvp9 post (snippet only, blogs.gnome.org unreachable) says ffvp9 beats libvpx's VP9 decoder by 25-50 percent single-threaded and about 25 percent at two threads in 2017 (Phoronix snippet). A Bitmovin snippet says libvpx-vp9 uses CPU time more efficiently than x265 but Bitmovin and the Bultje 2015 encoder-speed section were unreachable (ENOTFOUND), so no numbers could be opened. | w10-mine-l |
| libwebsock | https://github.com/JonnyWhatshisface/libwebsock | Easy-to-use and powerful web socket library. | Repo page and raw README return 404 (also payden/libwebsock); no benchmark found in WebSearch (two queries), so no numbers against any baseline could be reached. | w5-mine-m |
| libxls | https://github.com/libxls/libxls | Read binary Excel files from C/C++ | No numeric comparison. README covers building and the xls2csv tool only; web searches for libxls timings against xlrd, calamine or Apache POI found none, and the ExcelBench suite does not include it. | w3-mine-f |
| libxmi | https://gnu.org/software/libxmi/ | Function library for rasterizing 2D vector graphics | No numeric comparison reachable. gnu.org, ftp.gwdg.de and t2linux.com were EGRESS_BLOCKED, so the project page, manual and ChangeLog could not be read; no GitHub mirror was found. Web search snippets from distro package pages (SlackBuilds, Gentoo, OpenPorts, Source Mage, GnuWin32, GNU directory) describe it only as a C/C++ library that draws 2-D primitives (wide polylines, circular and elliptical arcs, polygons) into a user-supplied pixel matrix, with no speed claim and no benchmark against Cairo, Skia, AGG or any other rasterizer. | w1-mine-w |
| libxml++ | https://github.com/libxmlplusplus/libxmlplusplus | An XML Parser for C++. | No numeric comparison found. It is a C++ wrapper over libxml2 (LGPL-2.1, 81 stars); the README is build and install notes, the repo has examples/, tests/ and docs/ with no benchmark directory, NEWS has no performance entries, and the libxmlplusplus.sourceforge.net page makes no performance claim. No third-party shootout including libxml++ was reachable; at best its speed is libxml2's, and libxml2 is recorded separately. | w4-mine-j |
| libxo | https://github.com/Juniper/libxo | Allows an application to generate plain text, XML, JSON and HTML output using a common set of function calls. The application decides at runtime what output style should be produced. | No performance claim at all. README, doc/*.rst (faq, intro, howto, formatting, options, example) and the repo (347 stars, BSD-2-Clause) contain no benchmark or numbers; the project sells a single format-string API for four output styles, not speed. A search for xo_emit against printf or cJSON found no published numbers. | w4-mine-j |
| lightmapper | https://github.com/ands/lightmapper | Single-file library for lightmap baking, using an existing OpenGL renderer. Public domain | Only self-comparison numbers, no external baseline. README.md (1.5k stars) says its 'selective hierarchical interpolation' option gives about a 3.3x speedup on the example scene and up to about 10x on larger lightmaps, but the comparison is the library with the option on versus the same library rendering every texel (the README does not give the exact setting of the reference run), with no hardware, compiler or timing table, and no other lightmapper (Blender Cycles, Unity or Unreal lightmappers, xatlas-based bakers) is timed. No benchmark repo or third-party shootout including it was found. The licence is not tagged on the repo page (README says public domain). | w1-mine-w |
| linalg.h | https://github.com/sgorsten/linalg | Single header, public domain, short vector math library for C++. | No numeric comparison anywhere reachable. README.md (968 stars, Unlicense) and linalg.h were grepped for bench, fast, speed, perform, simd and optimi: the README positions it as a lightweight alternative to GLM, Boost.QVM and Eigen on design grounds (consistency, readable syntax) and carries no timing; the four open issues are API requests. Third-party benchmark repos found by search (RasterDuck/vectormathbench, mfoo/Math-Library-Test, heilokchow/math_lib_benchmark, 29thnight/Mathematics) were checked for 'linalg' and 'linmath' and do not include it (vectormathbench covers GLM, DirectXMath, SimpleMath, Sony Vectormath, move::vectormath and Realtime Math). | w1-mine-w |
| linenoise | https://github.com/antirez/linenoise | A small self-contained alternative to readline and libedit. | README (4.4k stars, BSD-2-Clause, last commit 2026-05-02) compares only size and licence: about 1600 lines of code versus readline at 30k lines and libedit at 20k, no runtime, latency, startup or memory numbers. No benchmark directory in the repo (linenoise.c, linenoise-test.c, example.c only) and WebSearch found no third-party speed comparison. It is a line editor, not a lexer, so the guessed category was wrong too. | w3-mine-b |
| linenoise-ng | https://github.com/arangodb/linenoise-ng | A small, portable GNU readline replacement for Linux, Windows and MacOS which is capable of handling UTF-8 characters. | README (373 stars, BSD, archived 2020-10-28) lists features, build steps and portability only; no performance, size or memory numbers against readline, libedit or the original linenoise. Repo has include/, src/ and tst/ with no benchmark directory. WebSearch for line-editor benchmarks returned only qualitative descriptions. Line editor, not a lexer, so the guessed category was off. | w3-mine-b |
| linmath.h | https://github.com/datenwolf/linmath.h | A lean linear math library, aimed at graphics programming. | No numeric comparison anywhere reachable. README.md (1.1k stars, WTFPL, 701 bytes) only lists supported types (vec3, vec4, mat4x4, quaternions) and GLSL-compatible layout, with no speed text; the 14 open issues concern quaternion correctness, warnings, documentation and double support, none quote timings. The cglm README (the usual C comparison point) has no mention of linmath, and the benchmark repos checked (vectormathbench, Math-Library-Test, math_lib_benchmark) do not include linmath.h. | w1-mine-w |
| LiquidFun | https://github.com/google/liquidfun | A 2D physics engine for games. | Directional claim only, no numbers. The v1.1.0 release notes say the release 'speeds-up the simulation' and add ARM NEON optimization (b2ParticleSystem::UpdateContacts rewritten with NEON, optional 16-bit particle indices), with no measured gain and no named baseline. LiquidFun is Box2D plus a particle/fluid system; the programmer's guide only says its solver is 'high performance' and 'order N'. WebSearch found no benchmark against Box2D, Bullet or ODE. Repo archived by owner on 2026-02-13, last commit 2018-01-10, no repo-level README to check. | w8-mine-j |
| list.h | https://github.com/nbulischeck/list.h | Implementations for singly linked and doubly linked list functions. | No performance claim or number at all. README.md (52 stars, GPL-3.0, last commit 2024-02-24) documents the SL_ and DL_ macros only; src/list.h has no mention of fast, speed, bench or perf; tests/ holds 17 functional tests (test0.c to test16.c with .res files and run_tests.sh), no timing code. Web search for a comparison with utlist or sys/queue found nothing that mentions it. | w1-mine-w |
| Lithium | https://github.com/matt-42/lithium | Build high performance C++ HTTP servers without being a C++ expert. | Directional claim only. The repo README says 'Lithium HTTP server and SQL drivers are ranked #1 on the TechEmpower benchmark (composite score)' and docs/introduction.md says it is 'benchmarked againts 200+ other frameworks' at TechEmpower; neither gives a figure or a named runner-up. tfb-status.techempower.com and matt-42.github.io (project site) are EGRESS_BLOCKED, the TechEmpower FrameworkBenchmarks entry (frameworks/C++/lithium README and benchmark_config.json) only configures the tests, and the docs directory has no benchmark page. The HN thread by the author (item 24315285) that carries the actual comparison against actix-web and h2o is not reachable from this environment; the catalog already holds a tier B record for it in data/wave5/raw/w5-http-servers-registry.json. | w5-mine-d |
| LIVE555 Streaming Media | https://github.com/rgaufman/live555 | Multimedia streaming library using open standard protocols (RTP/RTCP, RTSP, SIP). | No numeric comparison. live555.com was unreachable (ENOTFOUND); the third-party GitHub fork rgaufman/live555 README has no benchmark (only a CPU-spin bug fix note). One WebSearch for LIVE555 vs GStreamer/ffmpeg/libVLC found only a 2015 stress-test paper (Hermes tool against the LIVE555 server, no baseline, numbers not in snippet) and unrelated GStreamer CPU reports. | w10-mine-l |
| LLGL | https://github.com/LukasBanana/LLGL | Low Level Graphics Library (LLGL) is a thin abstraction layer for the modern graphics APIs | No numbers. github.com/LukasBanana/LLGL (2.6k stars, BSD-3-Clause) README describes it only qualitatively as a thin abstraction layer, with no overhead measurement against native Vulkan, OpenGL or Direct3D. One WebSearch found no LLGL overhead benchmark (2016 Phoronix article raises overhead as an open question). Issue and PR titles (#217 Vulkan DebugDevice slowness, PR #261 D3D12 descriptor recreation) are internal perf fixes with no named external baseline. It is also a graphics abstraction layer, not an image or colour resampling library. | w10-mine-l |
| LMDB++ | https://github.com/bendiken/lmdbxx | C++11 wrapper for the LMDB embedded database library. | Header-only C++11 wrapper (lmdb++.h) over LMDB, public domain (Unlicense), 288 stars. The README makes only a design claim: the procedural interface 'is implemented entirely using static inline functions, so there are no hidden extra costs'. No benchmark, no measured overhead against raw LMDB, and no benchmark directory (root: etc, check.cc, example.cc, lmdb++.h, Makefile). A zero-overhead wrapper is not a faster implementation of anything; the only faster-than claims about LMDB itself belong to LMDB, which is listed separately. | w6-mine-d |
| log4cplus | https://github.com/log4cplus/log4cplus | A simple to use C++ logging API providing thread-safe, flexible, and arbitrarily granular control over log management and configuration. | No numeric comparison against any baseline. README and ChangeLog carry no timing claims; tests/performance_test/ is an internal smoke test (performance_test.at only checks for exit code and an initialisation warning) with no baseline. Third-party shootouts checked (MuggleWei/cc_log_benchmark, mattiasflodin/reckless doc/performance.md, RafaGago/logger-bench) do not include log4cplus; three WebSearch queries found no published log4cplus numbers. | w11-mine-j |
| Log4cpp | https://log4cpp.sourceforge.net/ | A library of C++ classes for flexible logging to files, syslog, IDSA and other destinations. | No numeric comparison. Project page has only qualitative remarks (release 0.3.3 'new, faster and more complete' PatternLayout; zero runtime overhead when messages are compiled out) with no numbers or baseline. It appears only as a slow competitor target (spdlog bench/log4cpp-bench.cpp) and not in MuggleWei/cc_log_benchmark, Reckless or logger-bench result tables; searches found no published figures. | w11-mine-j |
| logfault | https://github.com/jgaa/logfault | A simple, elegant and efficient C++ header-only logging library. | No numeric comparison. README says it is 'optimized for moderate log-volumes' and that std::stream use is 'relatively slow compared to raw buffer-based IO'; doc/ holds only control_wrappers.md. Not present in MuggleWei/cc_log_benchmark, Reckless performance.md or logger-bench; a WebSearch found no benchmark including logfault. | w11-mine-j |
| LSHBOX | https://github.com/RSIA-LIESMARS-WHU/LSHBOX | A c++ toolbox of locality-sensitive hashing (LSH), provides several popular LSH algorithms, also support Python and MATLAB. | No numeric comparison against any named baseline reachable. The README only ranks LSHBOX's own eight schemes against each other without numbers ('Double-Bit Quantization Hashing and Iterative Quantization perform very good and are superior to other schemes'); the Python and MATLAB snippets note a second query takes 'about 3s' or 'about 0.5s' without re-indexing (self versus self). The repo root (include, matlab, python, tools) has no benchmark results, the LSHBOX-sample-data README only describes the audio dataset, and a web search found no third-party comparison against FLANN, LSHKIT, linear scan or other LSH libraries. It is also a GPL-3.0 toolbox for image retrieval, not a stdlib hash-map replacement, so the guessed hash-maps-sets category does not apply. | w1-mine-x |
| luacxx | https://github.com/dafrito/luacxx | C++11 API for creating Lua bindings. | No numbers anywhere. README only lists 'Low-overhead userdata and conversion support' as a focus and says it works with the Lua C API rather than hiding it. Not among the 15 binding libraries in ThePhD/lua-bindings-shootout (opened on github.com; baseline there is plain C Lua 5.3.4) and not found in the sol2 benchmark material surfaced by search. MIT, 164 stars, last commit 2026-03-13. | w12-mine-e |
| lwan | https://github.com/lpereira/lwan | Experimental, scalable, high-performance HTTP server. | No numeric comparison against a named baseline reachable. The README Performance section gives absolute figures only (about 320000 requests/second on a Core i7 laptop without disk access, about 290000 for files up to 16 KiB, 185000 for larger files, weighttp on the same machine) and quotes 'For Round 10, Lwan has taken the crown' (TechEmpower) and an InfoQ line without numbers. The TechEmpower Round 10 blog, InfoQ, lwan.ws and tfb-status are EGRESS_BLOCKED; the TechEmpower lwan README and benchmark_config list endpoints but hold no results; the-benchmarker/web-frameworks has no lwan entry; nanoant/WebFrameworkBenchmark has no lwan row; lwan issue search for nginx benchmarks shows no numbers. The catalog already has a tier B record for the Round 10 JSON result in data/wave5/raw/w5-http-servers-gh-cpp.json, taken from an archived copy of the blog that this session could not open. | w5-mine-d |
| lwIP | https://github.com/lwip-tcpip/lwip | A lightweight TCP/IP stack. | No speed comparison found. The README of the GitHub mirror (the listed savannah.nongnu.org page is EGRESS_BLOCKED) states only that the focus is reduced RAM use for systems with 'tens of kilobytes of free RAM and room for around 40 kilobytes of code ROM' and lists a 'raw/native API for enhanced performance', with no numbers and no named competitor. lwIP is the incumbent baseline in other projects' comparisons (see the Mongoose record in w5-mine-d.json), not a challenger. | w5-mine-d |
| LZSSE-SIMDe | https://github.com/nemequ/LZSSE-SIMDe | A portable SIMD implementation of LZSSE compression. | README only shows parity with upstream LZSSE when SSE4.1 is native (LZSSE2 compress 87.18 s vs 86.33 s, decompress 0.55 s both; LZSSE8 decompress 0.45 s vs 0.44 s, avg of 5 runs on raspbian-jessie-lite-20151121.tar.xz, g++ 10, no CPU model) and slower numbers when SSE4.1 is emulated (-msse2 decompress 13.70 s vs 0.55 s). It states upstream cannot run without SSE4.1, so no baseline numbers exist, and makes no faster-than claim. It is a SIMDe port of the already catalogued LZSSE (ConorStokes/LZSSE), whose own faster-than-LZ4-HC evidence is the lzbench row. | w9-mine-b |
| m2cgen | https://github.com/BayesWitnesses/m2cgen | A CLI tool to transpile trained classic ML models into a native C code with zero dependencies. | No numeric comparison reachable. The README has no performance or benchmark section (FAQ covers recursion limits, unpickling and float64 only), the repo page shows no benchmark material, and the READMEs of dmlc/treelite, siboehm/lleaves and kossisoroyce/timber do not mention m2cgen. Web searches found no benchmark of m2cgen-generated C against scikit-learn, XGBoost or LightGBM predict; one search snippet pointed to a paper that used m2cgen output (arXiv 2411.10143) but arXiv is blocked here and it was not opened. It is a code generator (a Python tool that emits C), not a faster implementation of a library facility, so no named baseline applies. | w1-mine-x |
| MADNESS | https://github.com/m-a-d-n-e-s-s/madness | Multiresolution Adaptive Numerical Environment for Scientific Simulation. | No numeric comparison against any named baseline found. The README (GPL-2.0, 221 stars) describes capabilities, conda packages and downstream projects (TiledArray, MPQC, Tequila, FrayedEnds) and has no benchmark section; the pymadness README path 404s. A search for MADNESS timings against NWChem or Gaussian-basis codes found only accuracy discussions (multiwavelet vs Gaussian basis accuracy), not speed. The SISC 2016 paper (arXiv 1507.01888) and the readthedocs and doxygen sites are on blocked hosts, so any comparison inside them could not be checked. | w8-mine-d |
| magic_get | https://github.com/apolukhin/magic_get | std::tuple like methods for user defined types without any macro or boilerplate code. | No numeric comparison found. The repo is a mirror of Boost.PFR (boostorg/pfr, BSL-1.0, 216 stars, last commit 2023-10-11); README and doc/pfr.qbk only compare code size and usability (hand-written db customization vs PFR) and describe the implementation, with no benchmark directory (test/ has config, core, core_name; misc/ has generators). Searches for PFR compile-time or runtime benchmarks found only documentation. Other reflection libraries (for example enchantum, qlibs reflect) use PFR or magic_enum as the baseline they beat, so no win by PFR exists in reachable sources. boost.org and the Boost mailing list were blocked. | w4-mine-j |
| Magick++ | https://github.com/ImageMagick/ImageMagick | ImageMagick program interfaces for C++. | C++ API layer of ImageMagick (Magick++/ directory in ImageMagick/ImageMagick). Project and README make no speed claim. No shootout row exercises Magick++ by name. Best reachable data: libvips Speed-and-memory-use wiki (Threadripper PRO 3955WX, Ubuntu 25.04, run 12 Aug 2025; load/crop/shrink/sharpen/save of a 10000x10000 TIFF) shows ImageMagick 7.1.1 convert/magick at 4.44 s/4.41 s versus libvips 0.57 s, GraphicsMagick 2.05 s, OpenCV 3.15 s, i.e. ImageMagick slower than every fast incumbent; the only baselines it edges are FreeImage 4.65 s, libgd 5.47 s, netpbm 6.06 s, G'MIC 11.63 s, Octave 35.1 s, and nobody claims that as a win. Not recorded. | w10-mine-m |
| magnum | https://github.com/mosra/magnum | Lightweight and modular C++11/C++14 graphics middleware for games and data visualization. | README has no benchmark. Magnum and Corrade changelogs (doc/changelog.dox, corrade-changelog.dox) contain only qualitative 'faster' remarks (std::strtoull alternatives, normal matrix) and one self-comparison (TextureTools::DistanceField 60% of previous time); no numbers against a named external baseline. No third-party shootout found. | w10-mine-b |
| mailio | https://github.com/karastojko/mailio | mailio is a cross platform C++ library for MIME format and SMTP, POP3 and IMAP protocols. | README (11.8 KB) and repo page have no benchmark, timing or performance claim; no comparison against libcurl, vmime or any other mail library. Search found no third-party benchmark that includes it. Library is Boost.Asio-based with MIT licence, 485 stars, last commit 2026-09-23. | w5-mine-n |
| Maximilian | https://github.com/micknoise/Maximilian | C++ Audio and Music DSP Library. | README, repo page and docs list features only; no benchmark, speed claim or comparison with STK, JUCE, Faust or Web Audio nodes. WebSearch found the Goldsmiths 'Write once run anywhere revisited' paper (asm.js via Emscripten) and the ICLC 2024 maximilian.js paper; their hosts (eprints-gro.gold.ac.uk, ualresearchonline.arts.ac.uk) were unreachable (ENOTFOUND) and search snippets show no performance numbers. | w10-mine-m |
| mbed TLS | https://github.com/Mbed-TLS/mbedtls | Another crypto implementation. Apache-2.0 | No claim or number that Mbed TLS is faster than a named baseline. The README (raw.githubusercontent.com/Mbed-TLS/mbedtls) has no performance statement and only mentions OpenSSL as a test-script peer; the project ships a benchmark program that prints absolute rates. Where comparisons exist Mbed TLS is the slower side: search snippets of a wolfSSL blog (posts.wolfssl.com, blocked here, so not opened) report wolfCrypt 15.7x faster for AES-256-GCM, 5.0x for SHA-256 and 49.3x for ECDSA P-256 verify on an i9-11950H, and another snippet says OpenSSL does a TLS handshake about 18x faster than Mbed TLS (source not opened). Speedups quoted for Mbed TLS itself (about 30 percent faster handshakes from 2.22.0 to 3.2.1, user report on the blocked trustedfirmware list) are self versus self. Mbed TLS targets small footprint, and the guessed concurrency-queues-locks category does not fit a crypto and TLS library; tls.mbed.org was not opened. | w1-mine-x |
| MediaInfoLib | https://github.com/MediaArea/MediaInfoLib | Convenient unified display of the most relevant technical and tag data for video and audio files. | README is a licence stub (BSD-2-Clause) with no speed claim. WebSearch found only anecdotes (ffprobe slow for 1000+ files versus exiftool, FFmpeg trac #7381) with no MediaInfo timings and no benchmark against ffprobe or exiftool. No numeric comparison reachable. | w10-mine-m |
| memory | https://github.com/foonathan/memory | STL compatible C++ memory allocator library. | No numeric comparison against std::allocator or any other baseline reachable. The README, doc/index.md and CHANGELOG.md contain no numbers (the changelog only says 'improved performance of pool allocators'), the repo tree has no benchmark directory (tool/ holds node_size_debugger and test_types only), and discussion #154 has the maintainer telling a user to benchmark memory_pool<array_pool> themselves. Web search snippets only repeat the tagline ('blazingly fast', 'optimized for performance using AVX2', constexpr heavy) with no numbers and no named baseline (GLM, Eigen, DirectXMath), and a follow-up search for an omath benchmark found nothing. | Onigmo | https://github.com/k-takata/Onigmo | Fork of Oniguruma, supporting more advanced regexps. BSD-2-Clause. | No numeric comparison reachable for Onigmo itself. Issue titles on performance (#100 'performance regression if quantifier lower bound is 1', #97 'Performance problem with /k/i and /s/i', #151 add API to mitigate ReDoS) have no PCRE comparison numbers. The only shootout found with PCRE2 numbers, rust-leipzig/regex-performance, measures upstream kkos/oniguruma master and not Onigmo (recorded under Oniguruma); Onigmo's engine is a fork of Oniguruma 5.9.x with different code, so those rows are not transferable. Last commit 2024-06-15, 679 stars, BSD-2-Clause. | w1-mine-z |
| oof | https://github.com/s9w/oof | Convenient, high-performance RGB color and position control for console output. | README only advises batching output and notes that printf and std::cout are slow on Windows (directional claim, no numbers); tests/ and demos/ contain no timing comparisons; no shootout against rang, termcolor or raw ANSI escapes found. | w10-mine-b |
| Open MPI | https://github.com/open-mpi/ompi | Message passing interface implementation. BSD-3-Clause. | No first-party numeric comparison against a named baseline reachable. The repo (2.7k stars, BSD) README and docs (history.rst, index.rst, tuning-apps) only state a goal of 'extremely high, competitive performance' with no numbers; www.open-mpi.org (papers page) and laro.lanl.gov (IPDPS 2006 'Infiniband scalability in Open MPI', snippet said latency up to 10% better than MVAPICH) are EGRESS_BLOCKED, so that paper could not be opened. Third-party results found are mixed and not claims of being faster: open-mpi/ompi issue #11765 (MPI_Allgatherv of 40 million ints per process, Open MPI 5.0.x with UCX) shows Open MPI 5.153137 s vs Cray MPICH 0.724109 s on 32 nodes (about 7x slower) and only 4.941182 s vs 7.161596 s on a single node, closed as not planned; pmodels/mpich issue #7188 reports MPICH slower than Open MPI in a GPU ping-pong but gives graphs and no numbers. Open MPI is the incumbent others compare against, so no record. | w1-mine-z |
| Open Watcom | https://github.com/open-watcom | Watcom C, C++, and Fortran cross compilers and tools. | Opened the org page, open-watcom-v2 repo page and README, the open-watcom-v2-wikidocs docs listing (compiler guides, library references, no benchmark page), and FrenkelS/djdoom (a Doom DOS compiler shootout of DJGPP, Digital Mars, CC386 and Watcom whose README gives no results); also FastDoom and Doom8088 READMEs. Any speed evidence belongs to individual implementations. | w11-mine-k |
| OpenDDS | https://github.com/objectcomputing/OpenDDS | An open source C++ implementation of the Object Management Group (OMG) Data Distribution Service (DDS). | README and repo pages have no performance numbers or baseline. performance-tests/bench holds tooling only. | OpenH264 | https://github.com/cisco/openh264 | Open Source H.264 Codec. | No first-party benchmark: README, wiki (7 API pages), RELEASES and repo page opened, none contain speed numbers or a comparison with x264/FFmpeg. Third-party evidence found points the other way. cisco/openh264 issue #2962 (Apr 2018, opened): 1920x1080, 600 frames, single thread, OpenH264 209.7 s vs x264 via FFmpeg 54.711 s (x264 about 3.8x faster, private build, a maintainer suspected -O0); issue #2067 (opened): OpenH264 1080p best 12 fps vs x264 'up to 40fps' on a 1.4 GHz Core i5, x264 preset unstated, maintainer says the gap is 'very little' at equal quality in Baseline profile. A WebSearch snippet of a 2018 libav-user/x264-devel post reported OpenH264 '4 times faster' (54.7 s vs 13.6 s) but its 54.7 s equals the x264 time in issue #2962, so the snippet looks reversed or mislabelled; those mailing-list pages are blocked here and were not opened. A mendevi (readthedocs, blocked, snippet only) dataset page says libopenh264 used less energy than libx264 at equal resolution and quality, with no numbers seen. BSD-2-Clause, 6.2k stars. | w10-mine-n |
| OpenImageDenoise | https://github.com/OpenImageDenoise/oidn | High-performance, high-quality denoising library for ray-traced images. | Directional claim only. The project site openimagedenoise.org is blocked here. Third-party search snippets (not opened) show OptiX denoiser faster than OIDN-CUDA, so no win over a baseline found. | w10-mine-c |
| OpenImageIO | https://github.com/OpenImageIO/oiio | Powerful image and texture wrangling library with support for a wide number of common lossy and RAW formats. (awesome-cpp, Image Processing; licence tag Modified BSD) | No numeric comparison against another library. The repo has moved to https://github.com/AcademySoftwareFoundation/OpenImageIO (Apache-2.0, 2.4k stars); its README makes no speed claim. CHANGES.md (raw.githubusercontent.com) quantifies speedups only against OIIO's own earlier code: IBA::resample 'speed up 20x or more' (PR 4993, jessey-git table 4096x4096 uint8 PNG 25% resize 282.5 ms main versus 16.9 ms PR, CPU and compiler not stated), resample axis tables 1.9-3.6x (PR 5395, Windows 11, MSVC 14.50, CPU not stated), NEON exp/log 2.5x, maketx --envlatl over 10x. PRs 4993 and 5395 name no OpenCV, ImageMagick or libvips baseline. Two WebSearch queries (oiiotool versus ImageMagick, OIIO versus OpenCV/libvips/OpenEXR) found no head-to-head timing that includes OIIO. Lead: a run of imageinout_test (PR 5037, read/write speed versus tile size) or a libvips vips-bench style harness with OIIO added would give numbers. | w10-mine-o |
| OpenJPEG | https://github.com/uclouvain/openjpeg | An open-source JPEG 2000 codec written in C language. (awesome-cpp, Image Processing; licence tag BSD-2-Clause) | No numeric win over an outside baseline. README says the repository is unmaintained and makes no speed claim; wiki pages Optimization and WhatsNew (opened) have only qualitative notes. Numbers exist only against OpenJPEG's own earlier revisions: PR 786 (multithreaded T1/DWT decode, i5 750, Release, 4 threads: 730 to 359 ms and 3920 to 1310 ms on its test files versus the previous PR 783 revision), PR 945 (T1 flag optimizations, total 23049 to 20172 ms, -12.5%, CPU and flags not stated), PR 1253 (forward DWT, i7-6700HQ, 5-3 vertical pass 7.9 s to 1.7 s). PR 917's Travis log (benchmark script, reference column labelled Kakadu but run.sh sets REF_VERSION=v2.1.2, so the reference may be an older OpenJPEG) shows the then-new build 149.9% slower than the reference, and a CNRS HTJ2K page (merovingio.c2rmf.cnrs.fr, seen only as a WebSearch snippet, not opened) says OpenJPEG is 10x (lossy) to 15x (lossless) slower than Kakadu; a 2007-2010 Second Life mailing-list anecdote (OpenJPEG 0.051 s versus GeoJasPer 0.074 s versus Kakadu 0.025 s) came only from search snippets and list-archives.secondlife.com and ffmpeg.org (2013 libopenjpeg versus FFmpeg native decoder thread) could not be opened (getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND). Guessed baseline libjpeg is the wrong format. Lead: Second Life 2007 sldev post if the host opens (would be tier C anecdote versus Jasper). | w10-mine-o |

| OpenSubdiv | https://github.com/PixarAnimationStudios/OpenSubdiv | Pixar's library for evaluating and rendering subdivision surfaces on CPU and GPU (awesome-cpp, Graphics; licence tag Modified Apache2) | No numeric comparison against an outside baseline. Other release notes (3.1 to 3.7), far_overview.rst, osd_overview.rst and bfr_overview.rst have no timings. | OpenVDB | https://github.com/AcademySoftwareFoundation/openvdb | Library and tools for storing, editing, and rendering volumetric datasets (awesome-cpp, Graphics; licence tag MPL2, now Apache-2.0) | No usable numeric comparison against an outside baseline in reachable sources. README has no speed claim. doc/faq.txt and doc/nanovdb/FAQ.md (raw.githubusercontent.com) say OpenVDB is 'almost always superior' to dense grids 'in our experience' and NanoVDB is 'generally faster' than OpenVDB, with 'only a benchmark comparison can tell you', all without figures. doc/points.txt: 2x to 3x from spatially organised point data versus randomly ordered linear data (internal layout comparison). The root CHANGES file has numbers only against OpenVDB's own earlier code (box filter 10-50x faster, mesh-to-volume 1.5x to 5.5x faster, CRC32 6.5-17x) plus one claim against an outside baseline in release 0.104.0 (February 15 2013): '.vdb files are now saved using a mask-based compression scheme that is an order of magnitude faster than ZLIB and produces comparable file sizes for level set and fog volume grids', with no workload, hardware or method (would be tier C at best, a file-format codec claim, not a data-structure claim). The Museth 2013 ACM TOG paper (research.dreamworks.com), the NanoVDB 2021 talk paper (research.nvidia.com), artifacts.aswf.io, www.openvdb.org and developer.nvidia.com all failed with getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND; arXiv is blocked. Lead: Museth 2013 paper tables versus dense grids and Field3D, and NanoVDB 2021 CPU/GPU timings versus OpenVDB, if those hosts open. Category guess color-and-resampling is wrong (sparse volumetric data structure). | w10-mine-o |

| PF_RING | https://github.com/ntop/PF_RING | High-speed packet processing framework. | README says 'from 80k pkt/sec on a 1,2GHz ARM to more than 20M pkt/sec per core on a low-end 2,5GHz Xeon' and 'enables you to capture packets faster', with no baseline, methodology or benchmark code. doc/*.rst in the dev branch (examples, zc, vanilla, modules) show sample pfcount output only (e.g. 14.2 Mpps / 9.55 Gbps on a ZC interface) and no libpcap comparison. Web search snippets mention PF_RING versus vanilla libpcap comparisons (17% to 58% gains; a Czech MUNI survey and an ISP RAS 2021 paper, plus ntop Cardigliano.pdf and Deri's Ring.pdf), but those hosts (muni.cz, mathnet.ru, ntop.org, luca.ntop.org, edn.com) are blocked in this environment, so nothing could be opened or checked. Worth retrying with open network access: ntop.org PF_RING vs libpcap pages and the 2021 packet-IO frameworks survey. 3.0k stars, GPLv2 kernel and LGPLv2.1 userland, active (dev branch). | w5-mine-e |

Support/Benchmarks/Http README describes a tool comparing AsyncWebServer with Python's built-in HTTP server, but its numbers (71.01 vs 45.23 requests/sec) are a placeholder 'Example Output' block (AsyncWebServer built in Debug in the instructions), not a measured result. FAQ mentions Support/Scripts/BenchmarkStdCppMode.sh (normal against strict build, 'not a CI performance threshold'), self against self. Two searches found no third-party shootout that includes sc_map. Directional claim only. | w1-mine-ac |
| SDL | https://github.com/libsdl-org/SDL | Simple DirectMedia Layer. | No numeric comparison showing SDL faster than a named baseline. The SFML-vs-SDL benchmark repo found has no results text, and a gamedev.net benchmark of SDL 1.2/2/3, HGE and SFML seen in search snippets was blocked so its numbers were not seen. SDL is a platform layer that other projects benchmark against rather than a faster replacement for a named incumbent. | w10-mine-q |

The README says SDS 2 'may be slower with certain workloads' and argues better cache locality from a single allocation, but gives no numbers. Related PRs: #101 (SDS 2 changes) claims up to 10x speedup on 32-bit ARMv7 for sdsll2str by using int instead of long long, which compares against the previous SDS code, not std::string or another library, with no method stated; #16 (case-conversion tweak) was closed with no benchmark data. One WebSearch for SDS versus std::string append numbers found none. Directional claim only; also a C library, so std::string is not a like-for-like baseline. | w1-mine-ac |
| sebsjames/maths | https://github.com/sebsjames/maths | A templated C++20 math library which emphasizes convenience and pleasure for the client programmer (used in mathplot). | No numeric comparison against any named baseline. Directional description only, and the claim words list is empty. | w1-mine-ad |

Leads that were blocked here (EGRESS_BLOCKED) or only seen as search snippets: Rahn et al. 2018, Bioinformatics 34(20):3437 'Generic accelerated sequence alignment in SeqAn using vectorization and multi-threading' (doi 10.1093/bioinformatics/bty380); AnySeq (arXiv 2002.04561, IPDPSW 2020) reports SeqAn 2.4.0 faster than AnySeq and Parasail for score-only AVX512 and both ahead of Parasail with traceback, no numbers seen; Suzuki and Kasahara, BMC Bioinformatics 2018 doi 10.1186/s12859-018-2014-8 Table 1 lists 'SeqAn ED' 7.97 GCUPS vs Parasail 0.69 and Edlib 13.23 (snippet only, different algorithms, SeqAn not the claimant). Search snippet of the Git commit that added the DC_SHA1 knob (mirror pages not opened) reports it slower than the incumbents: 1.75x slower than block-sha1 and 2.7x slower than OpenSSL SHA-1 (packfile hash 3.580s sha1dc vs 2.046s block-sha1 vs 1.335s OpenSSL). The repo's benchmarks/stdgpu/*.inc programs (Google Benchmark style; unordered_map, unordered_set, vector, deque, bitset, mutex) time stdgpu containers only, with no baseline library. The only candidate numbers are in the author's paper 'stdgpu: Efficient STL-like Data Structures on the GPU' (arXiv 1908.05936, cited in the README), but arXiv, ar5iv, alphaxiv and hgpu.org were EGRESS_BLOCKED and WebSearch snippets held no numbers. Category fit is also doubtful: GPU hash containers belong to hash-maps-sets, not thread-pools-work-stealing. Retry with arXiv access. | w11-mine-d |

Not a speed project. | w1-mine-ad |
| STLport | https://sourceforge.net/projects/stlport/ | An exemplary version of STL. | Directional claims only, no numbers on any page that could be opened. The project site www.stlport.org did not resolve. WebSearch snippets point to a 2010 LibreOffice developer-list post (libstdc++ against STLport 4.5, native STL usually faster except for large maps and some std::string cases) and a forum anecdote of a 25 percent whole-application gain on HP-UX; neither page could be opened, and the first is mixed rather than a win. | w11-mine-m |

A search snippet attributes to the talloc docs 'about 4% slower than malloc on an x86 Debian box' (page not opened, and it is a slowdown). ccan tal benchmark speed.c (tal vs talloc vs malloc) also has no committed results. The README says 'designed for performance' with no numbers. contrib/ruby/script/benchmark is a benchmark-ips script that compares trilogy with mysql2 (connect, escape, ping, query, large query) but the repo publishes no results; issue #7 (Benchmarks and experience from prod?) has no numbers, only the author's recollection that execution and allocation performance were 'slightly better' than mysql2; PR #212 (Speed up Trilogy#escape, 239.7k to 1.039M i/s) compares old vs new Trilogy, not another client; issue #224 reports a slowdown vs mysql2. A third-party dev.to benchmark post exists but dev.to is blocked here (getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND), so no mysql2 numbers could be opened. | w6-mine-h |
| TSMP | https://github.com/fabian-jung/tsmp | An intrusion and macro-free C++20 library for static reflection. It uses libclang to extract reflection data from your source code and makes it usable via template specialization. | No benchmark, number or baseline anywhere reachable. Readme.md (257 lines) has no performance section; repo tree (doc has only Changelog.md, test, examples, tooling) has no benchmark directory. Readme says the library is not production ready. A web search for reflection-library comparisons found none that include it. | w4-mine-m |
| Tufão | https://github.com/vinipsmaker/tufao | An asynchronous web framework for C++ built on top of Qt. LGPL2. | README says only 'High performance standalone server' (opened via raw.githubusercontent.com and github.com page; 595 stars, maintainer says can no longer maintain it); no numbers, no benchmark, no comparison. One WebSearch for Tufão benchmarks found only Cutelyst TFB posts that do not mention Tufão. Directional claim without numbers, project not notable enough for a tier D record. | w5-mine-q |
| Tulip Indicators | https://github.com/TulipCharts/tulipindicators | Library of functions for technical analysis of financial data. | Lead worth a retry from an unrestricted network. Repo has benchmark.c, a harness that times each indicator against TA-Lib when built with -DTA_LIB (make benchmark_talib; generated results go to docs/benchmark.php), and the README says 'Uses fast algorithms', but the repo holds no result numbers. The results page lives on tulipindicators.org (benchmark page), which is EGRESS_BLOCKED here, so no numbers were read. LGPL-3.0, 943 stars, last commit 2024-01-08. Baseline would be TA-Lib. | w1-mine-af |
| tungsten | https://github.com/tunabrain/tungsten | A high-performance physically based renderer in C++. | Not a numerics library: it is a physically based path/photon-mapping renderer (zlib licence, 1.8k stars) built on Embree. The README only says it 'tries to offer good performance through frequent benchmarking and optimization' and gives no numbers or comparison against Mitsuba, pbrt, Cycles or any physics engine; the guessed baseline (ODE or Bullet Physics) and category (blas-lapack) do not apply. A search for render-time comparisons found none (only an OpenBenchmarking test profile that runs Tungsten alone). | w8-mine-f |
| twitc | https://github.com/sinemetu1/twitc | Mini library for interacting with the Twitter OAuth API. MIT. | README (opened via raw.githubusercontent.com) describes a liboauth + json-c Twitter API client; no performance claim or benchmark. Not an HTTP server; the guessed category and baseline were wrong. | w5-mine-q |
| txiki.js | https://github.com/saghul/txiki.js | A tiny JavaScript runtime. | No source claims txiki.js is faster than any baseline, and the only numbers found show it not faster. README (opened) says 'small and powerful', built on QuickJS-ng and libuv, with no performance claim. benchmark/ holds only hashing.js (native mbedtls hashing versus npm JS hash packages, printed at run time, no stored results; the commit that added it is 'hashing: replace JS implementation with mbedtls based one', 2026-03-03) and million-timers.js (no baseline). The one third-party suite that includes it, ahaoboy/js-engine-benchmark (v8-v7 suite, nightly run of 10/5/2026 on GitHub-hosted runners, CPU not stated), scores txiki.js 819 on Ubuntu versus QuickJS 1217 (slower), quickjs-ng 647 (ahead, but txiki.js embeds quickjs-ng so this is a build difference) and Hermes 1530 (slower); on macOS arm64 txiki.js scores 1218 versus QuickJS 1761 and quickjs-ng 1660 (slower than both); its footprint there is 4.1M versus QuickJS 1M. The same pattern as the Duktape and JsonCpp no-evidence records. If the orchestrator wants a not-faster record, the rows to use are the Ubuntu and macOS arm64 tables in https://github.com/ahaoboy/js-engine-benchmark (59 stars, nightly CI commits through 2026-10-05). txiki.js itself: MIT, 3.2k stars, last commit 2026-10-01. | w12-mine-c |
| uClibc-ng | https://github.com/wbx-github/uclibc-ng | ) - Small C library for developing embedded systems. | Project claim is small size, not speed ('much smaller than the GNU C Library'); the GitHub mirror README and description have no benchmark or numbers, and uclibc-ng.org is EGRESS_BLOCKED. Listed URL https://uclibc-ng.org/. Guessed category sequence-containers is wrong (it is a libc, libc-replacement). LGPL-2.1, 215 stars. | w1-mine-af |
| universal | https://github.com/stillwater-sc/universal | A C++14 header-only library implementing arbitrary posit arithmetic. The posit number system is a tapered floating point that is more efficient than IEEE floating point. Posits enable reproducible computational science. | No speed claim against a named baseline. The README states only general benefits of tailored number systems (speed-ups of two to three orders of magnitude for deep learning formats such as half precision and bfloat16, not measured on this library) and a 20% AVX2 build-flag gain; benchmark/README.md just links openbenchmarking.org and the repo has no published comparison with MPFR, GMP, Boost.Multiprecision or SoftFloat. Search snippets of the project's own paper (arXiv 2012.11011) say its software posits and reference implementation are slower than hardware IEEE float, SoftFloat and MPFR. It is a collection of number systems (posit, fixed-point, cascade/multi-component floats, arbitrary integers), not a big-integer library; the guessed category does not fit. | w8-mine-f |
| upb | https://github.com/protocolbuffers/upb | A small protobuf implementation in C. | Speed claim is directional only: README says 'comparable speed to protobuf C++, but is an order of magnitude smaller in code size'; the wiki says it parses 'at roughly the same speed' as built-in C++ parsers. The only numbers found are code size, in archive/docs/vs-cpp-protos.md: .text 26Ki for upb vs 187Ki for C++ lite and 983Ki for C++ full (trivial parse and serialize of descriptor.proto), which is not a speed win. archive/benchmarks/ (benchmark.cc, compare.py, google/benchmark with bytes-per-second counters) is public but no result numbers are published in the repo; the 2021 tail-call blog post with GB/s numbers (blog.reverberate.org) is EGRESS_BLOCKED. Repo archived 2025-12-09, development moved to protocolbuffers/protobuf/upb; 1.5k stars. Retry candidate if numbers on the blog or in upb commit messages become reachable. | w1-mine-af |
| urho3d | https://github.com/urho3d/Urho3D | Cross-platform rendering and game engine | No numeric comparison anywhere reachable. Repo archived 2023-01-25. | w10-mine-s |
| uriparser | https://github.com/uriparser/uriparser | Strictly RFC 3986 compliant URI parsing and handling library. | README says only 'cross-platform, fast'; ChangeLog, issues and pull requests carry no benchmark numbers; ada-url/ada benchmarks and boostorg/url repo pages show no uriparser comparison; uriparser.github.io was egress-blocked. | userver | https://github.com/userver-framework/userver | Asynchronous C++17 framework with a rich set of abstractions and database drivers for fast and comfortable creation of efficient microservices, services and utilities. | Only comparative evidence is scripts/docs/en/userver/framework_comparison.md, which says userver 'comes ahead of its competitors from the table' in TechEmpower run 3c2e9871-9c2a-4ff3-bc31-620f65da4e74 and shows scripts/docs/img/top15.png: a rank list with userver 13, actix 14, vert.x 15, officefloor 16, drogon 17, vertx-web 18, jooby 19, axum 20. That is ordinal rank only, with no composite scores, requests per second, hardware or flags in the repo, and the techempower.com results page is blocked here (EGRESS_BLOCKED), as are Yandex and Medium release posts. The rest of the docs (intro, http_server, pg driver, redis, grpc) carry no speed numbers. Worth retrying with TechEmpower access: composite and per-test scores for userver versus drogon (C++) in that run and in later rounds (Round 23). 3.0k stars, Apache-2.0, active. | w5-mine-e |
| utf8.h | https://github.com/sheredom/utf8.h | Single-header UTF-8 library, designed to mimic C-style string | No performance claim or numbers anywhere reached. The README documents a string.h/strings.h-style API (utf8len, utf8cmp, utf8str and others) and a Unlicense dedication; the repo has only a test directory (no benchmark), and a search of its issues and pull requests for performance, benchmark, slow and faster found only a MSVC compatibility PR (#124) and an early-exit PR (#57) with no figures. A web search for utf8.h versus strlen, strstr or other UTF-8 libraries returned nothing about this project. Design goal is convenience, not speed. | w1-mine-h |
| utf8proc | https://github.com/JuliaLang/utf8proc | ) - Library for processing UTF-8 data. | No win found. bench/ holds bench.c, icu.c and unistring.c harnesses (eprun Deutsch/Japanese/Korean/Vietnamese files, NFKC, -O2) but the repo publishes no results. Search snippet of issue #12 (page comments not rendered by WebFetch) reports utf8proc slower than ICU: Deutsch_.txt 0.0039 s vs 0.000112 s, Japanese_.txt 0.0046 s vs 0.00027 s (ICU given a preallocated buffer). No libunistring numbers reachable. MIT, 1.3k stars. Snippet-only, so no record. | w1-mine-af |
| utf8proc | https://github.com/JuliaStrings/utf8proc | A clean C library for processing UTF-8 Unicode data. | No claim that utf8proc is faster than anything, and no recorded numbers. The README describes a 'small, clean' normalization/case-folding library with no speed statements. The repo has a bench/ directory (bench.c for utf8proc, icu.c for ICU4C unorm2 NFKC, unistring.c for libunistring, using the eprun Deutsch/Japanese/Korean/Vietnamese test files, built with -O2) but no checked-in results; bench/icu.c notes its own ICU run preallocates a huge output buffer. Issue #12 ('benchmarking/profiling', 2014) only asks for a benchmark against ICU; its page body showed no comments, although a search snippet attributed ICU-faster timings to it (Deutsch 0.00390216 s utf8proc vs 0.00011213 s ICU, unverified snippet only, and in utf8proc's disfavour). Search snippets (pages not opened) say other projects that use utf8proc as a baseline, for example NuC8, and the Ruby utf8_proc gem versus UNF, report it slower, not faster. Directional comparison only and not in its favour. | w1-mine-h |
| uvw | https://github.com/skypjack/uvw | C++ wrapper for libuv. | README (opened via raw.githubusercontent.com) and repo tree (docs, src, test, subprojects; no bench directory) hold no benchmark, no number and no comparison with libuv, libevent or Asio. It only says 'tiny and easy to use' and that it stays true to libuv's API. The issue search for benchmark, performance or overhead returned no issue with such a title or figures. A web search found no published uvw overhead measurement. Nothing to report. | w5-mine-r |
| V7 | https://github.com/cesanta/v7 | An embedded JavaScript engine. | README claims small size (40k-120k static, 800 bytes RAM at init with freeze) and makes no speed claim; no benchmark in README, ReleaseNotes.md or the javascript-zoo engine page. Deprecated in favour of mJS; last commit 2020-11-30; GPL-2.0 or commercial. | w12-mine-f |
| v8pp | https://github.com/pmed/v8pp | Header-only library to expose C++ classes and functions into V8 to use them in JavaScript code. README.md and docs/{addons,config,context,convert,exceptions,index,plugins,utilities,wrapping}.md have no benchmark, faster or overhead statements; the test/ directory holds functional tests only (no bench or perf files, no timing code); issue search for benchmark/performance/overhead/slow returns one unrelated question (#31, arrays of structs). The website (pmed.github.io) is blocked by the egress proxy. Two web searches found no benchmark of v8pp against raw V8 API or other binding libraries; the only number found is a search snippet of a Chinese-language PuerTS article (cloud.tencent.com) that reports PuerTS static methods 50-90% faster and member methods 4-5x faster than v8pp, i.e. v8pp as the slower baseline, from a competing project's authors and not opened. | w12-mine-g |
| vector.h | https://github.com/swenson/vector.h | Header library for typed lists. | No numeric comparison anywhere reachable: the repo has only vector.h, demo.c, Makefile and a README with usage text, no benchmark files and no open issues; web searches found no shootout or post benchmarking it against std::vector or std::list. | w1-mine-ag |
| Veles | https://github.com/Samsung/veles | Distributed platform for rapid Deep learning application development. | No numeric comparison anywhere reachable. | Verovio | https://github.com/rism-digital/verovio | Verovio is a fast and lightweight music notation engraving library. | README calls it fast, portable and lightweight but has no timings and no comparison with LilyPond, MuseScore or any other engraver. Project book, ISMIR 2014 paper and W3C mailing list posts were EGRESS_BLOCKED, so any numbers inside them could not be checked. Directional claim only. | w10-mine-d |
| VexCL | https://github.com/ddemidov/vexcl | A C++ vector expression template library for OpenCL/CUDA. | No published numbers reachable. README, docs/index.rst and docs/talks.rst contain no comparison. examples/benchmark.cpp (VexCL vs Boost.Compute sort and scan, keys/sec), examples/thrust-sort.cpp (vex::sort vs thrust::sort, 16M ints, 10 timed passes) and examples/fft_benchmark.cpp (FFTW, clFFT, cuFFT) are benchmark programs only, with no result tables in the repo and no hardware or flags stated. Issues list (benchmark-related #38, #123, #150, #210) shows no timings. Peer-reviewed papers do compare it but are blocked here (EGRESS_BLOCKED / ENOTFOUND for arxiv.org, ar5iv, epubs.siam.org, hgpu.org, academia.edu, emergentmind.com): Parallel Processing Letters sort and scan comparison, arXiv 1601.03144 (VexCL 1.3.1 vs Boost.Compute, Thrust, CUB, Modern GPU, CLOGS, Bolt on AMD R9 270, NVIDIA GTX 480 and K40), and Demidov, Ahnert, Rupp, Gottschling, SIAM J. Sci. Comput. 35(5) C453, arXiv 1212.6326 (VexCL vs Thrust, ViennaCL, MTL4 on ODE integration). WebSearch snippet of 1601.03144 only: VexCL best of the OpenCL libraries for scan below about 2M elements on the R9 270, but below 20% of CUB throughput at the largest sizes, so not a win over CUB. Paper-access retier lead (tier B, domain numerics-and-science / GPU computing, not thread-pools-work-stealing); needs the actual tables before any record. | w11-mine-o |
| vfs-pipe | https://github.com/Nouridin/vfs_pipe | One-header library to expose internal C variables as a virtual filesystem for live inspection and tuning. | Directional claim, no numbers. The only quantified claim is the DASIP 2014 paper abstract (seen only in WebSearch snippets: code up to 32x faster than naive equivalents), a hand-written naive loop baseline rather than a named library, and the PDF hosts (perso.ensta-paris.fr, hal) are unreachable here. Last commit 2019-02-07. | w10-mine-s |

Only claims are qualitative ('efficient single-threaded event loop', 'minimal overhead') plus Autobahn RFC 6455 conformance badges, which measure compliance, not speed. The only numeric speed statement is in ChangeLog ('Speed up software CRC-32 computation by a factor of 1.5 to 3'), which compares against an earlier zlib release, not a named external baseline. In the lzbench 2.4 table zlib 1.3.2 -1 (89.0 / 297 MB/s) is slower than zlib-ng, libdeflate, LZ4, Snappy, LZO and zstd, so no one claims it wins. | w9-mine-b |
| Zopfli | https://github.com/google/zopfli | A compression library that performs very good but slow deflate/zlib compression. | Slower than its baseline by design, so no faster-than record. The README (Apache-2.0, 3.6k stars; folders go/, rust/, src/) says it performs 'very good, but slow, deflate or zlib compression' and carries no numbers. The only figures found are from Google's 2013 announcement 'Compress data more densely with Zopfli', seen only as a WebSearch snippet because developers.googleblog.com is blocked here: output typically 3-8% smaller than zlib at maximum compression, at 2 to 3 orders of magnitude more CPU time; third-party posts in the same search (blocked) give 17x to 100x slower than gzip -9. That is a ratio claim against zlib with a speed penalty, the same shape as the cmix and heatshrink no-evidence cases. In the catalogue, Efficient Compression Tool (fhanau/Efficient-Compression-Tool) already carries the only Zopfli timing seen (from catalogue data, not re-opened here): enwik8 gzip output, zopfli -i30 390.6 s against ECT -5 25.1 s (15.6x derived), with Zopfli the slow side. | w9-mine-d |

| ZZIPlib | https://github.com/gdraheim/zziplib | Provides read access on ZIP-archives. | No numeric comparison against any baseline found. Opened and checked: github.com/gdraheim/zziplib (70 stars, COPYING.LIB, no releases listed), raw README.MD, DEVGUIDE.md, TODO and ChangeLog from the develop branch (only grep hits: 'compression based solely on free algorithms provided by zlib', 'ninja should be faster than makefile-build' about the build system, and 'rewrite pagesize/mapoff calculations - this should be faster in itself', a 1.26k-line ChangeLog note with no numbers), and the project site zziplib.sourceforge.net (only 'speed up dat-file development dramatically', about developer workflow). Issue #147 'Faster lookup of entries in large file-count Zip' is a feature request by a user: reading the central directory of a >1M-entry archive costs 'double digit MB' versus about 40MB for regular unzip, an idea for a sorted alternate index with no measurement of zziplib against minizip, libzip or any other library. Two WebSearch queries (zziplib vs minizip/libzip/miniz read speed; zziplib faster/slower benchmark) found no shootout or third-party timing that includes zziplib; only package descriptions calling it 'intentionally lightweight'. The library is a read-only ZIP access layer over zlib, so it is not expected to beat zlib or minizip, and the project makes no speed claim. | w9-mine-e |
